# Training, from three doors — plan

**Status:** walked 2026-09-14, drafted and attacked 2026-09-17, not started. Follows `training-rebuild.md` (2026-09-09), whose ten
phases were executed on branch `training-rebuild` between 2026-09-09 and 2026-09-11. This plan is
what is still wrong after that work, found by using the feature rather than reading about it.

**Approved 2026-09-17 — "allow everything".** Every recommendation in this document is taken:
the 8 blockers, the 27 questions below, and the 68 open questions inside the phases. The executor
does not re-ask. What that authorises, named here so none of it is silent:

- **Deletions.** Both fill-in-afterwards forms and the "Anything else" tab (Q4), the tap-to-build
  program builder (Q19), the blue-grey kit `src/programs/components/ui.tsx` (Phase 9), the
  duplicate "Training" row in Quick Actions (Q17), `ProgramPicker` (Phase 5), and the Life Mastery
  catalogue/builder/running-band components (Phase 8). All are code, none is your data.
- **Database.** Additive migrations only (program-write functions, a `started_at` on the finish,
  `timezone_source`, two summary columns, a distance bound), plus the one **permission change**:
  locking the program's computed columns so only the engine can write them (B8 / Q12), done at the
  end of Phase 1. No row of yours is deleted by any migration.
- **App-wide.** The bordered-button fix in `components/ui/button.tsx` changes every outline and
  ghost button in the app, not only training (Q16); the bottom tab bar grows a quarter-pixel and
  its labels go from 11 px to 12 px on every page (Phase 9); the phone zoom rule in
  `globals.css` becomes `!important` with the large-input exception (Phase 9).

Any single one can still be vetoed before its phase runs — say which, and the phase's step is
struck rather than the whole answer re-opened.

---

# Part 1 — For you (plain language)

## What this is answering

You said: *"Go check the workout function. I know of 3 places it exists — in the template step,
in the workout step (both in Life Mastery), and in the tracking place. Make sure it is actually
fully functional for real users, by walking through it. I have also asked many times to update
how it looks — I think it looks like shit; on the tracking page, when I want to use it, it is just
not functional at all."*

So this plan answers two questions: **does it work**, and **why does it look wrong** — and then
says exactly what to build so that both answers change.

## How it was checked

- I signed in as the two test accounts and walked every screen on a phone-sized browser
  (390 × 844, iPhone 14) and on a desktop one: the Tracking page, the Training page and all four
  of its tabs, the live workout from Start to the finish summary, History, Progress, and the Life
  Mastery Templates and Systems steps including "Build my own". 50 screenshots are in
  `.playwright-mcp/walk/`; the write-up is Appendix A.
- One account had a year of seeded training (144 workouts), the other started with none.
  After the walk, each account was put back the way I found it, through the app's own delete
  buttons — no direct database writes.
- In parallel, ten reading agents audited the code behind each door, an independent agent tried
  to refute every one of their findings by opening the cited lines, and a final agent looked for
  what all of them had missed. 112 findings went in; 108 survived refutation (107 traced in code,
  1 plausible); 4 were thrown out. The critic added 9 more; I added 9 from the browser.
  **126 findings**, every one with a file and line, are in Appendix B; the phases below close
  all of them or say why not.
- Three visual designs of the four surfaces were drawn independently, scored by two judges, and
  merged into the one spec in Part 3. Nothing in it is a new style: every element is an existing
  component of this app.

## The honest answer first: what already works

The core loop is real and it holds: pick StrongLifts on the Life Mastery Templates step →
"Start tracking this" → the Tracking page shows "Today: Workout A · 3 lifts" → Start → the live
screen has every set as a row with a ✓ → tick, and the rest clock starts by itself → add a lift
the rack forced on you → Finish → "Done. 1 minutes · 2 sets · 200 kg" with what the program
will ask next time → History has the session, collapsed to "2 × 20 kg × 5" → the program's
weights moved the right way. The database holds exactly what the screen said. No console errors
anywhere, nothing scrolls sideways, and every request that failed during the audit showed a
"could not load" line rather than pretending.

That is the machinery from the last plan, and it is sound. What follows is what sits on top of it.

## The five things that actually hurt

**1. The Tracking page has no door when you need one — and on your account something worse.**
The training card is built to disappear "when there is nothing to say": with no program running
and no workout open it renders **nothing**, and the only trace of training on the page is the
sixth row of Quick Actions, below the fold. When there *is* a program, the card's button is a
black outline that reads as disabled, sits flush against the "Edit tiles" link, and appears a beat
after the page so the tiles jump down.

**What your own account actually shows** (read-only probe, 2026-09-17 — this replaces the guess
that was here before): you are on Upper / Lower, started 15 July, two sessions logged, timezone
Europe/Copenhagen. And **a workout has been open since Friday 11 September with no sets in it**.
So the card is not missing — it says *"A workout from Friday is still open"* with "Finish it or
throw it away", and every Start on every screen is refused underneath, because the database allows
one open workout at a time. Worse, that workout cannot be saved as it stands: the finish screen
defaults its end time to now, calls it a 144-hour workout, and disables Save — the way out is the
"Change" link and typing an end time, or Discard. **That is the "not functional at all" you
reported, and no phase below fixes it retroactively — it is two taps to clear, see the closing
note.** Phases 0 and 3 stop it happening again: a workout open longer than six hours is named as
Friday's on the card, the finish sheet opens with its times already expanded, and Start never
fails silently behind it.

**2. Some buttons do a different thing from what they say.**
- The Tracking card names one session and can **start another one**: it reads the weekday to
  name today, but the Start it sends leaves the day out, and the server then uses the program's
  own counter. Skip one scheduled day on a Mon/Wed/Fri week and Friday's card says "Legs" while
  Start opens "Pull" — and the wrong lifts progress.
- Finish a workout on one device (or lose the reply on gym wifi) and **every later Start on the
  other device is refused** with a raw database error, until you clear the browser's storage. The
  app remembers a key that the finished workout already used.
- "Start again" on a finished program shows **"Deleting…"** on the Delete button beside it.
- "Reset to start" warns that your weights go back to where you began. They do not; only the
  week does.
- A bodyweight lift (a pull-up, a dip) in your own week makes "Start tracking this" fail with
  "Validation failed", because one route refuses 0 kg while the shared rule allows it.
- Right after you finish Workout A, the card says **"Today: Workout B · Start"**. Nothing knows
  you already trained.
- Starting StrongLifts writes **"2 days a week"** into your Life Mastery plan. It is three.

**3. There are three ways to log the same workout, and they disagree.** Beside the live screen
the old "I did all of this — save it" form survives behind a fold on the Today tab (still with
45 min and effort 3 pre-filled), and a third form lives on the "Anything else" tab and on the goals
health panel. The third one records pounds as kilograms, throws away a set with a blank weight,
and calls a lift a "new PR" against a different window than the finish sheet uses. Three writers,
three rulebooks. You asked, once, for the form to go; it went behind a disclosure.

**4. The screens were assembled from three kits.** The Today tab alone has sky-blue tabs, an
orange Start, a green Save and six button styles; the Life Mastery block is a fourth language
(emerald), sits at the top of a **12,661-pixel** step, and shows the day name as "Wor…" because
four icon buttons take the row. Helper text is 10 px grey on black (2.6:1 contrast — unreadable).
Every input in the builder is 12.5 px, so an iPhone zooms the page when you tap one. Tapping the
Training tab in the bottom bar makes the bottom bar disappear.

**5. Nothing automated vouches for any of this.** CI has been red on `main` since 2026-09-09
(492 lint errors, none in training). On 2026-09-17, while this plan was being drafted, a lint
baseline landed in this tree (352 errors remain, frozen; only a *new* one now fails) — but both
CI workflows run only on `main`, so this branch, 23 commits ahead, has never been checked at all,
and no test has ever pressed Start on the Tracking card or walked Templates → Start → Training.
The previous plan's live-screen promises — swap a lift, reorder, warm-up set, editable rest, lift
history — are still unbuilt and were never tested for.

## What each screen becomes

The full spec — every element, every state, the numbers a browser test will check — is Part 3.
This is what you will see.

**The Tracking page always has a training card**, between "The one thing" band and the stat
tiles, never taller than a phone's thumb-reach. It says what today is and has one button that
does the obvious thing:

- no program: "No program running — Start a workout and add lifts as you go, or pick a program"
  with **Start a workout** and a "Pick a program" link;
- a session due: "Workout A · Squat · Bench Press · Barbell Row · Last time Fri, all 15 sets"
  with **Start workout** — and Start opens *that* session, never another;
- already trained: "Trained today — Workout A · 45 min · 14 sets · next is Workout B on Wed"
  with **See today's workout**;
- rest day: "Rest day — next is Workout B on Wed" with **Start Workout B early**;
- a workout running: an orange-edged card, "Workout A in progress · 23 min · 6 of 15 sets" with
  **Resume**; one left open for hours: "Monday's Workout A is still open" with **Finish or
  discard it**;
- two or three programs: the one you are most likely to train today, and a line "Also today:
  Run 2 · Sprint Triathlon";
- could not load: an amber line and "Open training", never a Start that might collide.

The button is orange-tinted, not a black box; the page keeps its one solid-orange "Start
Session". The duplicate "Training →" row in Quick Actions goes.

**The Training page's Today tab is today's workout, not a list of programs.** One card: the
program name, this week as seven dots (done · today · to come), the session name, its lifts,
one orange **Start workout**, and "Last time Fri · all 15 sets". A ⋮ on the card opens a sheet
with everything about the program — change it, put it on set days, skip a session, reset, end —
so the four grey buttons and the History fold under the card go. With several programs the card
shows the one due and an "Also running" list underneath; tapping a row swaps the card. Programs
themselves — running, yours (saved weeks, finished ones), the catalogue, "build your own" — move
to a **Programs screen** reached from the header. The tab strip becomes three real tabs (Today ·
History · Progress) in the app's own style; "Anything else" goes; the bottom tab bar is back on
this page. **Logging a past workout** is one button on History: pick when and which session, and
the normal live screen opens with that start time. The two old fill-in-afterwards forms are
deleted.

**The live workout screen becomes a real tracker.** Each set is SET · PREVIOUS · KG · REPS · ✓
with 44-pixel controls; tapping the set number marks it warm-up, working or drop set and takes
an effort score; swiping a set left deletes it. Each lift's ⋮ can swap the lift, move it up or
down, add a warm-up set, change its rest (and keep the change after the phone locks), and show
the last five times you did it. Supersets sit in one card with one rest after the pair. The
header counts mm:ss and "Finish" is the one orange. The finish sheet lists only prescribed lifts
as misses — a lift you added is never "0 of 3" — and lets you set both when you started and when
you finished. The summary is a receipt: minutes · sets · kg, "New best" only when there was
something to beat (otherwise "First time logged"), every set collapsed to "4 × 100 kg × 5", and
what the program will ask next time. The same receipt opens from History.

**Life Mastery stops hosting a second copy of the catalogue.** The Templates step gets one small
card: which program you are on, the week it prescribes ("Workout A · Workout B, in turn" — never
an invented "2 days a week"), when you last trained, and two links: change program, today's
session. Both open the Training page's Programs screen and bring you back here. The Systems
step's "Training week" card reads the same live fact from the program instead of a copy in the
browser, so a phone and a laptop can no longer disagree. "Build your own" keeps the "write your
week" text mode (which worked in the walk) as the one way to build.

**One visual language.** Everything above is built from the app's own buttons, cards, tabs and
inputs. The blue-grey training "kit" and the emerald Life Mastery buttons on training surfaces are
removed rather than repainted; no text under 12 px on a phone; no control under 44 px; one
solid-orange control per screen.

## What this deliberately does not do

- **No native app.** A locked iPhone still cannot buzz you when the rest ends. The rest bar says
  so; that stays.
- **No new visual style.** One language — the one Tracking already uses — applied to every
  training surface. The Life Mastery pages keep their own surface colour; the controls on them
  become the app's controls.
- **No second copy of the Life Mastery plan.** The plan keeps living in the browser; the fix is
  to stop copying the program into it and derive the training week from the database instead
  (Q9). Persisting the whole plan is a different project.
- **No AI.**
- **No change to the vice module**, the goals hub, or the tracking session flow.

## What it costs

Eleven phases, 169 steps, each with the file, the function and the test that proves it. Phases 0–2 are correctness and ship on their own: after them nothing lies and
nothing bricks, and the screens still look the same. Phases 3–8 build the four designed surfaces
and remove the duplicate logging paths. Phase 9 is the one-kit sweep the design phases build on
(its first step runs before Phase 3). Phase 10 is the proof — tests for every door and a CI that
is actually green. Part 4 gives the dependency order and which phases an agent team can run side
by side without editing the same file.

# Manual blockers

Each one was attempted before it was written down. "Attempted" means a command was run or a
screen was opened, and the result is quoted. Numbered so you can answer "1 yes, 2 skip".

### B1 — ANSWERED 2026-09-17. What your account shows, and the one thing to clear by hand.

*Attempted twice.* On 2026-09-14 the read-only probe of your account was refused by the
permission system, so every screenshot in this plan is from the test accounts and Part 1's
claim about your account was a guess. On 2026-09-17, with permission, it ran:

```
reachjvc@…  timezone Europe/Copenhagen, kg, 20 kg bar, 1.25 plate
  enrollment ACTIVE  upper-lower  beginner  kg  started 2026-07-15
                     cursor { cycle 2, week 1, dayIndex 0, sessionCount 2 }
  workout_logs 3   OPEN: started 2026-09-11 05:16Z, 0 sets
  drafts: "2", "12"
```

*What that means, in plain language:* you are on the Upper / Lower program with two sessions
logged, and **a workout you started on Friday 11 September is still open with nothing in it**.
One workout may be open at a time, so until that one is closed every Start — on Tracking, on
Training, from Life Mastery — lands you back on it instead of starting anything. And it cannot be
saved as it is: the finish screen guesses "ended now", makes that a 144-hour workout and disables
Save. The way out is on the workout itself: open `/programs/live`, press **Discard workout** (it
holds no sets, so nothing is lost), or press Finish → "Change" → type when you actually stopped.

*What I need from you:* nothing for the plan — this blocker is closed. One decision for your
account: **my recommendation is to discard it**, because it has no sets. Say the word and I will,
or clear it yourself in two taps. Phases 0 and 3 make the state visible and recoverable so it
cannot strand you again, but neither reaches back and cleans this row.

### B2 — CI has never run on this branch, and `main` is still red. **Needs you.**

*Attempted, twice.* On 2026-09-14: `npx eslint .` → 498 problems (492 errors). On 2026-09-17,
after commit `c8d7a901` ("Lint can be green again", made in this tree while the plan was being
drafted): `npx eslint .` → **358 problems (352 errors)**, and `npm run lint:ratchet` → "352,
none new", exit 0 — a baseline (`eslint-baseline.json`) now lets CI's Lint step pass unless a
NEW error appears. So the previous plan's Q7 is answered by that commit. What is still true:
`curl https://api.github.com/repos/reachjvc/Daygame_coach/actions/runs` → the last run on
`main` (2026-09-17 07:14, before that commit) is `failure`, and both workflows trigger only on
`main` and `beta` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:3-7`, `e2e.yml:3-7`) — this branch, 23 commits
ahead of `main`, has never been checked by CI at all. The baseline also excuses 11 lint errors
inside the training slice's own tests.

*Why it matters:* nothing automated has vouched for a week of training work, and nothing will
until CI runs on this branch.

*What I need from you:* the answers to Q7 (now: run CI on every branch?) and Phase 10's open
question 1. **My recommendation:** yes — Phase 10 step 2 changes the trigger to every branch, and
step 1 cleans the training slice out of the baseline so "none new" means something there.

### B3 — I cannot test a real iPhone.

*Attempted:* the training suite runs on WebKit at an iPhone-14 viewport, and my walkthrough used
the same engine. That is a real engine in a fake phone: no lock screen, no vibration, no Safari
zoom-on-focus, no Add-to-Home-Screen.

*Why it matters:* three findings depend on it — the rest timer after the screen locks
(live-workout-10), the 12.5 px inputs that make Safari zoom (design-system-04), and whether a
rest-end sound plays at all.

*What I need from you:* after Phase 6, one workout on your own phone: tick a set, lock the phone
for two minutes, unlock — say what the rest bar shows and whether tapping a weight box zoomed the
page. **My recommendation:** accept the limit; no native push in this plan.

### B4 — Another agent works in this tree, and the tree moved while this plan was written. **Do not answer; just know.**

*Attempted:* `git status` on 2026-09-14 → two untracked files (`supabase/migrations/20260914120000_drop_four_orphan_tables.sql`,
`tests/unit/navigation/testRoutesSealed.test.ts`). On 2026-09-17 both are committed (`d56d3ef7`),
plus three more commits landed on 2026-09-14/17: the lint ratchet (`c8d7a901`), a shared loader
`src/shared/useLoad.ts` (`d48620ae`), and a boot-file correction (`849172f5`). Untracked today:
`docs/plans/life-mastery-deployment.md` and `.main-check/` — not training work.
`supabase migration list --linked` → everything applied, nothing pending.

*Why it matters:* every phase runs `supabase migration list --linked` before pushing anything and
never pushes a migration it did not write; the executor must not commit, delete or "tidy" the two
untracked files. The phases were attacked against the 2026-09-17 tree, so they already know about
the loader and the ratchet; Part 1's walkthrough is from 2026-09-14 and the screens have not
changed since.

*What I need from you:* nothing. **My recommendation:** let that agent land its own work first.

### B5 — The test accounts are shared with the browser suite.

*Attempted:* eight Playwright specs wipe the seeded account (four times over, on four engines —
tests-and-ci-10). I walked on both accounts while no suite was running, and put each back
afterwards through the app's own routes (account A: 0 running programs, as found; account B: its
two never-trained June enrollments and its ended June StrongLifts, as found; the workout, the
enrollment and the saved week I created were deleted by the app's own Delete paths, and the
delete correctly rewound the program).

*Why it matters:* any manual check you or I do during a test run loses its program mid-set.

*What I need from you:* nothing now. **My recommendation:** Phase 10 creates a third training
account for manual walkthroughs (Q14).

### B6 — "Make it look good" needed a design, and now has one. **Needs your yes.**

*Attempted:* three independent layout proposals for the four surfaces (a tracker-first angle, a
door-first angle, a one-kit angle), scored by two judges on fidelity to the app's language,
hierarchy, honesty, phone usability, reuse, and whether they answer your complaint; the merged
spec is Part 3.

*What I need from you:* read the four layout blocks in Part 3 and disagree with specific lines.
**My recommendation:** say "build Part 3 as written" or name the lines to change; do not
re-open the style question — every element there already exists in the app.

### B7 — The repo's own instruction for reading the database does not work on this machine.

*Attempted:* `.claude/rules/database.md` says `supabase db query --linked` is the ground truth.
`supabase --version` → 2.75.0; `supabase db query --linked` → `unknown flag: --linked` (the
subcommand does not exist in this version). I read the database through a Node script that
loads the service key itself and never prints it; it is in the session scratchpad, not the repo.

*What I need from you:* nothing for this plan. **My recommendation:** update the CLI
(`v2.117.0` is current) when convenient, so the rule's command works again.

### B8 — One database rule needs your permission before it is written. **Needs you.** *(Phase 10 carries the exact lines and the two options as its ASK FIRST section.)*

*Attempted:* read `supabase/migrations/20260618_create_program_tables.sql:41` and the finish
function. A signed-in person can, with the browser's own credentials, write the program's
computed weights, its position in the program and its "finished" flag directly — bypassing the
engine and the double-finish guard (data-integrity-05). Nothing in the app does this; the door is
merely open.

*Why it matters:* the repo's rule is that earned or computed data is system-only, and that a
policy is not enforcement — a trigger is. Writing that trigger changes what the database enforces.

*What I need from you:* the answer to Q12. Phase 10 writes the migration only after it.

# Open questions

Each has a recommendation, so "go with your recommendations" is a complete answer. These are the
decisions that shape the whole plan. Each phase in Part 2 also ends with its own numbered open
questions (68 in all, each with a recommendation) — the ones only that phase's executor needs
answered; where one repeats a question here, the phase says so.

### Q1 — Should the Tracking page show training when you have no program?

Today the card vanishes. (a) Keep it hidden until a program exists. (b) Always show it: with no
program it says "Training — Start a workout" and "Pick a program".

**Recommendation: (b).** The door has to exist before you can walk through it; a card that only
appears once you have found the feature elsewhere is the failure you reported.

### Q2 — After you have trained today, what does the card say?

(a) "Today: Workout B · Start" (what it does now — the program's next session, immediately).
(b) "Done today — Workout A · next is Workout B", with Start as a quiet secondary for the day you
really do train twice.

**Recommendation: (b).** The card must know the same thing History knows.

### Q3 — More than one program running: allow it, and show what?

The database allows one running program per discipline, so a runner on Couch-to-5K and
StrongLifts legitimately has two. Today the card shows only the newest, the Today tab shows an
inventory instead of a session, and two of the test account's programs have been "running" and
never trained since June without any screen saying so.

(a) One program at a time, full stop. (b) Keep one-per-discipline; the card and the Today tab
list each running program's *session* (not the program), and a program not trained for 14 days
gets a line offering to end it.

**Recommendation: (b).** It is what the schedule already promises; the fix is to show it.

### Q4 — Delete the two after-the-fact forms?

"I did all of this — save it" (behind the fold on Today) and "Log a past workout" (Anything else
tab and the goals health panel). They are the third and second ways to write a workout, with
their own rules, and the source of eleven findings.

(a) Keep them and fix each. (b) **DESTRUCTIVE** — delete both; the live screen gains a "log a
past workout" mode (you set when it started and ended; the finish sheet already has the editor),
and History gets the "Log a past workout" entry. What is lost: the one-tap "exactly as
prescribed — save" for a session you did not log live.

**Recommendation: (b).** One writer, one rulebook, one retry key. The one-tap save can come back
later as a pre-filled live screen if you miss it.

### Q5 — Where does the Life Mastery program block live?

Today it is the top of a 12,661-pixel step. (a) Leave it inline and shorten it. (b) Its own
screen inside Life Mastery, reached from the Fitness area on the Templates step and from the
Training week card on the Systems step; the step itself shows one card: what is running, and the
way in.

**Recommendation: (b).** A program picker with an editor inside it is a screen, not a paragraph.

### Q6 — One visual language, including inside Life Mastery?

(a) Training screens use the app's tokens (orange primary, the Card/Button components); the Life
Mastery block keeps the emerald/zinc kit so it matches its page. (b) One kit everywhere: the Life
Mastery surface keeps its background, but every control on it is the app's control.

**Recommendation: (b).** The `tone` prop that already exists on `RunningPrograms` is the shape
of the fix, applied once to the kit rather than per component.

### Q7 — CI: run it on every branch, and add the database tests as a step? *(the lint half of the old Q7 was done on 2026-09-17)*

Today both workflows run only on `main` and `beta`; the 16 database test files under
`tests/integration/db/` have never run in CI; ten Playwright projects are run by no job.

**Recommendation: yes to all three** — Phase 10 steps 2–3. Lint is already a ratchet (352
baselined, "none new"); Phase 10 step 1 proves the ratchet with a test and empties the training
slice out of the baseline.

### Q8 — "Reset to start": should the weights reset, or the words change?

The button warns "your current weights go back to where you began"; the code rewinds the
calendar and keeps every weight (`programRepo.ts:489` records `weights: false`).

**Recommendation: change the words.** Resetting weights destroys months of progression on a
mis-tap; rewinding the week is the useful half. Say "Start the program again from week 1 —
your weights are kept."

### Q9 — The Life Mastery training week: copy or derive?

Starting a program copies day names and a day count into the browser's plan, which is how
"2 days a week" happened and why the plan differs between your phone and your laptop.

(a) Keep copying and fix each copy. (b) The plan stores only the enrollment id; the days, the
count and the name are read from the enrollment every time they are shown.

**Recommendation: (b).** One representation of one fact. The Systems step's day editor then edits
the program's schedule (the same PUT the week strip already uses) instead of a copy.

### Q10 — A new account's timezone is UTC until they open Settings. How does it get set?

Onboarding used to detect it; onboarding was removed on 2026-09-08. Every "today" in training
is therefore computed in UTC for a new user — including the test account with 144 workouts.

**Recommendation:** on first sign-in, if the profile is still on the default and the browser's
zone differs, save the browser's zone through the existing Settings update and say so once
("Your timezone was set to Europe/Copenhagen — change it in Settings"). Settings stays the owner.

### Q11 — Is a first-ever set a "New best"?

**Recommendation: no.** A best needs something to beat; say "first time logged". One shared
definition of a best replaces the three the screens use today (finish sheet, Progress, the old
form).

### Q12 — Lock the program's computed columns against direct client writes? **(permission change)**

**Recommendation: yes, as a trigger** that refuses changes to `exercise_state`, `cursor` and
`is_active` from the authenticated role except through the finish function and the engine's
own routes — because a policy is advisory and a trigger is not. Written only after your yes.

### Q13 — Two icons are reused outside their registered role.

`Timer` (registered for the time tracker) on the rest bar; `CheckCircle2` (registered for
scenarios "completed") on the old session widget. **Recommendation:** approve `Timer` for "rest"
in the registry; the widget goes in Phase 4, so the second one needs no decision.

### Q14 — A third test account for manual walkthroughs?

**Recommendation: yes.** `test-user-c@daygame-coach-test.local`, created by the executor, never
touched by the suite, so a walkthrough during a test run does not lose its program mid-set.

### Q15 — Rest day: offer Start anyway?

**Recommendation: yes** — "Rest day · next is Workout B on Wed" with a quiet "Train today
instead" that opens the day picker. A rest day is advice, not a lock.

---

## Design decisions carried from Part 3

The design panel raised twelve of its own. Where one repeats a question above it says so; the
rest are new. Same rule: each has a recommendation.

### Q16 — Fix the app's bordered button once, for every screen.

The bordered ("outline") button paints itself the page's black on a slate card, and hovering
any bordered or transparent button flashes red — that is why the card's "Start" looked disabled.
The fix is one line in the shared button component and changes every such button in the app
(Settings, Goals, everywhere). **Recommendation: yes** — it removes the class of fault. The
training card works without it (it uses the orange-tinted style), so this can be said no to
without blocking anything.

### Q17 — Remove the "Training →" row from Quick Actions on the Tracking page.

The card above the fold is the door; the row 2,800 px down is a duplicate. **Recommendation:
remove.**

### Q18 — Two new fields in the workout API. *(same decision as Q4's replacement)*

`startedAt` when starting and when finishing (so past and stale workouts get their real times),
and a per-lift rest time saved on the workout (so an edited rest survives the phone locking).
**Recommendation: yes;** "log a past workout" cannot exist without them.

### Q19 — DESTRUCTIVE — delete the tap-to-build program builder (about 1,000 lines) and keep only "Write your week".

The written mode did the job in the walk and already handles supersets; the structured mode is
the single largest source of the old blue-grey styling. **Recommendation: delete.** If no, it is
repainted at roughly the cost of the rest of the Life Mastery phase combined.

### Q20 — No "Failure" set type.

The database allows warm-up / working / AMRAP / back-off / drop; "failure" would need a migration.
**Recommendation: skip it** — "Drop set" plus the effort slider cover what a lifter marks.

### Q21 — The receipt page must be built first.

`/programs/workout/[id]` does not exist; every "See today's workout" button and the finish
summary render it. **Recommendation:** it is the first step of Phase 6 and Phase 3 depends on
it (Part 4 order).

### Q22 — Icon registry: `Timer` gains "rest countdown", `Calendar` gains "history calendar toggle". *(extends Q13)*

**Recommendation: approve both.** No swap/reorder/history icons are added; menu rows are text-only.

### Q23 — Tracking's "Resume" is no longer solid orange.

The page keeps one orange ("Start Session"); a running workout is an orange-edged card with a
tinted "Resume". **Recommendation: tinted;** if you disagree it is one class and a documented
exception.

### Q24 — The week strip shows this week only, as dots, with no legend.

**Recommendation: yes.** Older training lives in History and Progress.

### Q25 — Life Mastery no longer starts programs itself. *(same decision as Q5 + Q9)*

Picking, editing, ending and building happen on the Training page; the Life Mastery step shows
a status card and links there and back; the plan stores only which program is linked.
**Recommendation: yes** — it is the only way to have one copy.

### Q26 — `/programs` gets the bottom tab bar; `/programs/live` does not.

**Recommendation: yes.** The Training tab currently lands on a page with no tabs.

### Q27 — DESTRUCTIVE — the after-the-fact forms also leave the goals hub's Health panel and the `/test/health` page. *(the rest of Q4)*

`WorkoutLogger` is mounted in three places. **Recommendation: all three go;** the Health panel
keeps the training card.

---

# Part 2 — Execution

## Conventions for whoever runs this

1. **Every step names the file and the exported symbol.** If a step does not say which function to change, it is a defect in this plan — say so rather than guessing.
2. **Every deliverable names its test.** A step is done when its named test passes *and* that test fails if the change is reverted. Prove it by reverting once.
3. **Run after every step, not at the end:** `npm test`, `node scripts/typecheck-ratchet.mjs`, and `npx eslint <the files you touched>`. The type ratchet must say "none new"; lint is measured against the baseline Phase 10 step 1 writes (until then, against `npx eslint <files>` on the touched files only).
4. **These guards must stay green and must never be loosened to pass:** `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts`, `routeReachability.test.ts`, `backNavigation.test.ts`, `writeCoverage.test.ts`. An allowlist may shrink; it may never grow.
5. **DESTRUCTIVE steps are marked and are not run without the answer to the question they name.** Anything marked ASK FIRST is a question to the owner, never a step.
6. **The working tree is shared with another agent.** Never `git stash`, never `supabase db push` without `supabase migration list --linked` first, never touch `supabase/migrations/20260914120000_drop_four_orphan_tables.sql` or `tests/unit/navigation/testRoutesSealed.test.ts`, and re-read a file before editing it if you did not just write it.
7. **Verify in a browser, not only in a test.** Dev server on localhost:3000; the production build on :3200 is the only way to see production-only behaviour. Screenshots go in `.playwright-mcp/`; delete the throwaways.
8. **New browser specs that start workouts or change programs go in the `training` project** in `playwright.config.ts`, or they race the one shared account.
9. **Whose clock:** every "today"/"this week" on the server comes from `getUserTimezone` (`src/db/settingsRepo.ts`); `new Date()` decides nothing about a day.

## Phase 0 — Starting and finishing a workout cannot brick, lie or open the wrong day
**What you can do after:** Tap Start anywhere and you get the session the card named — after a missed day, after finishing yesterday's workout on your laptop, after a dropped connection, and even if you tapped Start in two tabs at once — and you never see a raw database message. Save a workout on bad gym signal and the app checks with the server whether it went through instead of telling you "nothing was finished"; a run or a stretch counts as a run or a stretch; a blank weight box on a barbell lift is never quietly saved as 0 kg, while a pull-up with nothing added still saves; a lift you added on the day is never called a miss; and "New best" means your best ever, the same on every screen, and is never awarded on the first set you ever logged. Nothing here changes how a screen looks — the later phases do that on top of this.
**Why it is in this order:** every later phase rebuilds the screens on top of the start/finish path, and a prettier screen on a start that can brick is a prettier brick — so the data layer is made honest first and nothing here changes a layout.
**Findings this phase closes:** tracking-card-start-opens-wrong-day, programs-today-01, data-integrity-02, programs-today-04, live-workout-01, data-integrity-01, data-integrity-10, tracking-card-error-copy-contradicts-todaycard, data-integrity-09, critic-end-or-displace-program-while-workout-open, data-integrity-04, live-workout-05, after-the-fact-logging-06, walk-04-added-lift-counted-as-miss, live-workout-04, history-progress-01, after-the-fact-logging-05, critic-three-definitions-of-a-personal-best, walk-05-new-best-on-first-set

**Dropped inside this scope (one line each):**
- `critic-live-run-is-an-empty-weights-session` — half of it (a live run stored as "weights") is closed here under data-integrity-04, and the crash that stops a live run from even opening is fixed in step 1; the other half is a *distance* box on the finish sheet and a "Run · 5.2 km · 31 min" History row, which are the S3 screen (Phase 6) and the History screen (Phase 7). The design spec S3 has no distance box, so that half is Open question 1 rather than a step here. Phase 6 must add `distanceKm` to `FinishWorkoutSchema` and to `finish_program_workout` if the answer is yes; this phase leaves `finishWorkout` without a distance parameter on purpose so two phases do not both edit that function. (If Open question 4 is yes, Phase 6's migration must `CREATE OR REPLACE` the function with the `p_changes` and `p_records` parameters this phase added — it replaces the whole signature, so the executor of Phase 6 reads this phase's migration first.)

**Blockers:** No blockers. Checked 2026-09-17 by the attacker: the seven guard/hook test files this phase extends pass today (92/92 — `architecture`, `routeReachability`, `backNavigation`, `writeCoverage`, `useLiveWorkout`, `trainingCardState`, `healthService`); Playwright is 1.58 so `route.fetch()` + `route.abort()` can simulate a lost reply against the real server; `supabase migration list --linked` shows every local migration applied, including `20260914120000_drop_four_orphan_tables.sql`, which is now committed (it was untracked when the audit ran) — so the one optional migration in this phase (Open questions 4 and 5) can be pushed without shipping anybody else's work, PROVIDED the executor re-runs `supabase migration list --linked` first and stops if anything else is pending.

**Whose clock:** every "today" in this phase is `isoWeekdayInTimezone(await getUserTimezone(userId))` (`src/shared/dateUtils.ts:102`, `src/db/settingsRepo.ts:153`) — the account's timezone, never the server's `new Date()`. Every date printed on a record or a best is `toDateISO(toZonedDate(instant, timezone))` with the account's timezone, and step 6 removes the one place (`liftBests`) that would otherwise start stamping bests with the server's calendar the moment it moves server-side. The fact that the account timezone defaults to UTC until Settings is opened (critic-today-is-utc-until-you-find-settings) is Phase 1's; this phase makes sure there is exactly one place that asks, so Phase 1 fixes one call.

**Plain-language glossary for this phase:** a *start key* is a random tag the phone makes up before asking the server to open a workout, so that asking twice (because the first reply got lost) opens one workout, not two. A *race* is two of those requests arriving at the same moment. *Idempotent* means "asking twice does the same as asking once". A *migration* is a small file of database changes the executor applies to the live database. A *trigger* is a rule the database itself enforces, so even a script with full access cannot break it.

### Steps

1. **One rule decides which day "today" is, Start uses it, and a run can open its live screen.** `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `getTodaySession` (line 590) and new export `todaysSessionFor(userId, enr): Promise<SessionPrescription>`; `src/programs/programsService.ts` · new export `isSessionOf(program, dayId): boolean` (endurance schedules: any `weeks[].sessions[].id`; every other kind: `scheduleDays(schedule)` ids); `src/db/workoutRepo.ts` · `startWorkout` (lines 145–152) and `prescriptionForDay` (line 618). Today: `getTodaySession` fetches the enrollment, then picks the day by the account's weekday via `pickTodaysDay` (lines 606–620); `startWorkout` (lines 149–151) ignores that rule and falls back to `computePrescription(program, enr).dayId`, the cursor's day, so the Tracking card names Legs and Start opens Pull. Separately, `prescriptionForDay` (line 623) calls `scheduleDays(program.schedule)` unconditionally, and `scheduleDays` THROWS for an endurance plan (`src/programs/customize.ts:123–126`, "Endurance plans have weeks, not days") — so `/programs/live` crashes for a workout started on Couch to 5K (the critic finding was never run in a browser; this is why a live run "records nothing"). Change: extract the body of `getTodaySession` after the fetch into `todaysSessionFor` (the timezone read stays inside it — it is the one place that asks whose day it is); `getTodaySession` becomes fetch + `todaysSessionFor`. In `startWorkout`: when `input.dayId` is absent, `dayId/cycle/week` come from `await todaysSessionFor(userId, enr)` — on a weekday-pinned rest day that is the NEXT session flagged `restDay`, and starting it is the spec's "Start Workout B early" (S1 rest-day row), not an error; when `input.dayId` is present and `!isSessionOf(program, input.dayId)`, throw `StartRefused("unknown_day")` (step 2) instead of storing an unknown id; for an endurance program the cycle/week still come from the cursor. `prescriptionForDay`: for an endurance program return `computePrescription(program, enr)` and an empty `loadStyles` without touching `scheduleDays`; for every other kind unchanged. Delete the false comment at `src/programs/components/TrainingCard.tsx:66–67`. In `src/programs/programsService.ts` · `TrainingCardState` (line 1920) add `dayId: string` to the `today` and `rest` variants, filled from `prescription.dayId` in `trainingCardState`, and have `TrainingCard.startNow` (line 59) pass it through step 3's helper — so the card and the server agree even if a future caller forgets. *Test:* `tests/unit/db/workoutRepoStart.test.ts` — "starts the day the card named, not the day the cursor points at" (table-keyed fake Supabase client as in `tests/unit/health/workoutPaging.test.ts`, `vi.mock("@/src/db/supabase")` and `vi.mock("@/src/db/settingsRepo")` returning `Europe/Copenhagen`; a week pinned Mon/Wed/Fri with cursor on day 0, clock fixed with `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ["Date"], now: <a Friday 07:00 Copenhagen> })` → the inserted `program_day_id` is Friday's day), "an unanchored program still starts the cursor's day" and "a day that is not in the program is refused by name"; `tests/unit/programs/engine.test.ts` — "isSessionOf knows an endurance session id and refuses a made-up one"; `tests/unit/db/workoutRepoStart.test.ts` — "a Couch to 5K session opens its prescription without asking for days" (`prescriptionForDay` on an endurance enrollment resolves; reverting the `scheduleDays` guard makes it throw); `tests/unit/programs/trainingCardState.test.ts` — "carries the day id so Start opens the day it named". Relies on Phase 1 for the UTC-default timezone (critic-today-is-utc); relies on Phase 3 to rebuild the card's states on the widened `TrainingCardState`.

2. **A start is refused with a plain sentence, never with a Postgres message; a used-up start key cannot block the next start; two starts at once open one workout.** `src/db/workoutRepo.ts` · `startWorkout` (line 120) and new exported class `StartRefused extends Error { code: "already_open" | "start_key_spent" | "no_program" | "unknown_day"; status: 404 | 409; workout?: LiveWorkout }` (a class, not a type — `architecture.test.ts` "Type exports should only be in types.ts" greps `export type|interface`). Today: the key is only compared against a LIVE workout (lines 126–137); once that workout was finished elsewhere the same key is inserted again and the partial unique index `uq_workout_logs_client_key` (`supabase/migrations/20260907100000_one_workout_record.sql:115`) refuses it, and line 178 throws the raw `duplicate key value violates unique constraint …` text, which the route returns as 409 and every card prints. Two Starts racing hit `uq_workout_logs_live` the same way (line 178). Note a `client_key` row can ALSO be one that was never live: the after-the-fact writer `writeWorkout` (`src/db/programRepo.ts:869–883`) stores `client_key` on rows whose `started_at` is null — so "exists and `ended_at` is null" is not "open", and handing such a row to `toLive()` (`row.started_at!`, line 83) would be wrong. Change, in this order inside `startWorkout`: (a) `live = await getLiveWorkout(userId)` and `keyRow = select id from workout_logs where user_id = ? and client_key = ? maybeSingle()`; (b) if `keyRow && live && keyRow.id === live.id` → return `live` (this IS the retry); (c) if `keyRow` (any other row — finished, or never live) → throw `StartRefused("start_key_spent", 409)`; (d) if `live` → throw `StartRefused("already_open", 409, live)` carrying the open workout; (e) resolve program/day (step 1; `no_program` → 404, `unknown_day` → 409); (f) insert; if the insert fails with `error.code === "23505"` → re-read `getLiveWorkout` and, when it exists, throw `StartRefused("already_open", 409, thatWorkout)` — the same outcome as (d), so the client has ONE rule for "a workout is open: go to it" whether the second tap came a second later or in the same instant; if no live workout exists after a 23505 (the spent-key row appeared concurrently) throw `StartRefused("start_key_spent")`; any other insert error → `console.error(error)` and throw a plain `Error("Could not start the workout.")`. `error.message` from Postgres never reaches a caller of this function. `app/api/workouts/route.ts` · `POST` (line 18): `StartRefused` → `NextResponse.json({ error: e.message, code: e.code, workout: e.workout ?? null }, { status: e.status })`; anything else → 500 `"Could not start the workout."` (route is 17 code lines today; stays under 50). Messages: `already_open` "A workout is already open — opening it."; `start_key_spent` "That start was already used."; `no_program` "That program was not found."; `unknown_day` "That day is not part of this program." *Test:* `tests/unit/db/workoutRepoStart.test.ts` — "a key whose workout is already finished is refused as spent, not as a database error", "a key on a workout that was written after the fact is spent too, and is never handed back as live" (key row with `started_at: null`), "a lost reply retried with the same key returns the workout it opened", "two starts racing both end on the one workout, without a Postgres message" (fake insert answers `{ code: "23505", message: "duplicate key value …" }`, the live read then answers a row; assert `StartRefused.code === "already_open"`, `workout.id` is the winner's, and no thrown message contains "duplicate key"), "a day that is not in the program is refused by name"; `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` (already in the `training` project) — "two starts at once open one workout and never show a database message" (two POSTs with different keys via `Promise.all` from `page.evaluate`; exactly one live workout afterwards; one body is 201, the other 409 with `code: "already_open"` and the same `workout.id`; neither body contains "duplicate key").

3. **One helper starts a workout for every button, and it forgets the start key the moment the server answers.** `src/programs/hooks/useLiveWorkout.ts` · new export `startWorkoutRequest({ enrollmentId, dayId }): Promise<StartOutcome>` with `StartOutcome = { kind: "started" | "already-open"; workout: LiveWorkout } | { kind: "refused"; message: string } | { kind: "unreachable"; message: string }`; `startKeyFor` (line 423) and `clearStartKey` (line 440) stay. Today: `TrainingCard.tsx:59–81`, `TodayCard.tsx:60–92` and `StartLooseWorkout.tsx:38–61` each POST `/api/workouts` themselves, none clears the key after a successful start (so it outlives the workout — data-integrity-01), and the three disagree on the failure copy (TrainingCard says "nothing was started", which `TodayCard.tsx:83–89` explicitly rejected). Change: the helper is the ONLY place that POSTs `/api/workouts`. Rules: 201 → `clearStartKey(bucket)`, `started`; 409 `already_open` → `clearStartKey`, `already-open` with the server's workout; 409 `start_key_spent` → `clearStartKey`, mint a fresh key (`startKeyFor` after the clear) and retry ONCE, then apply these rules to the retry's reply (a second `start_key_spent` is `refused`, never a loop); any other 4xx → `clearStartKey`, `refused` with `body.error`; 5xx → keep the key (the row may exist — `unitFor` can fail after the insert; the next tap with the same key returns that row via step 2(b)), `refused` with "Could not start that workout."; network error → keep the key, `unreachable` with "Could not reach the server. Tap Start again — if it did go through, this opens that same workout." The three components call it and on `started`/`already-open` do `router.push("/programs/live")` (Phase 3 adds `?from=`); on `refused`/`unreachable` show `message` in their existing error slot (Open question 3 decides whether the card prints this sentence or the spec's fixed one). Delete the three hand-rolled fetches and TrainingCard's "nothing was started" sentence. Because `TodayCard.tsx` and `StartLooseWorkout.tsx` then contain no `fetch(`, remove both from `COMPONENTS_THAT_FETCH_THEIR_OWN_DATA` (`tests/unit/architecture.test.ts:653–654`) — "the fetching-screens allowlist only shrinks" fails otherwise; `TrainingCard.tsx` still fetches `/api/workouts/live` (line 90) and stays. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/useLiveWorkout.test.tsx` — "forgets the start key the moment the server answers, so a workout finished elsewhere cannot block the next start" (201 → `startKeyFor("e1")` returns a different key than before), "a key the server says is spent is replaced and the start retried once" (fetch called twice, with different keys, outcome `started`; a second spent reply → `refused`, fetch called exactly twice), "keeps the key when the reply is lost" (rejects → `startKeyFor` returns the same key), "keeps the key on a server error, because the row may exist", "goes to the open workout when the server says one is already running" (`already-open` carries the body's `workout`); `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — new test "only startWorkoutRequest posts to /api/workouts" (every `fetch(` whose first argument is the string `"/api/workouts"` or the template `` `/api/workouts` `` in `src/**` and `app/**` outside `src/programs/hooks/useLiveWorkout.ts` — offenders must be empty; no allowlist); `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` — "finished on another device, Start on this one still works" (context A: tap `start-workout` on /programs; context B = `browser.newContext({ storageState: "tests/e2e/.auth/user.json" })`: POST `/api/workouts/<id>/finish` for that workout; context A: tap Start again → URL `/programs/live`, no text "duplicate key"). Phases 3 and 5 rebuild the three components on top of this helper (testids unchanged).

4. **A lost finish reply is checked against the server, not asserted; Save is idempotent; a finished workout can always hand back its summary — the same summary it showed at the time.** `src/programs/hooks/useLiveWorkout.ts` · `finish` (line 334); `src/db/workoutRepo.ts` · `finishWorkout` (line 326) and new export `summaryFor(userId, workoutId): Promise<WorkoutSummary | null>`; new route `app/api/workouts/[id]/summary/route.ts` · `GET`; `app/api/workouts/[id]/finish/route.ts` · `POST`; `src/programs/types.ts` · `WorkoutSummary` (line 749) gains `unavailable?: true`, `changesUnavailable?: true`, `firstTimeLifts: string[]` (step 6); `src/programs/components/live/FinishSheet.tsx` · summary branch (line 100). Today: `finish_program_workout` commits before the reply travels back; on `!res.ok` (line 356) or a network error (line 363) the hook keeps the start key, keeps the workout on screen and says "Nothing was finished." — a guess, and wrong exactly when the signal drops; a second Save then gets "not open any more — reload" (`requireLive`, line 202), the summary is never shown, and (via the old key) every later Start is refused. Every finish error is a 409 today (`finish/route.ts:23`), including a real refusal such as "A workout cannot end before it started." — so "lost reply" and "refused" must be told apart by WHAT came back, not by the status. Change, server: (i) `finishWorkout` first reads the row by id and owner (`select id, ended_at, started_at`); if it does not exist → `Error("That workout no longer exists.")` (route: 404); if `ended_at` is set → return `summaryFor` — a retried Save is idempotent and ignores the retry's input; (ii) after the RPC, on ANY RPC error re-read the row: if it is now closed (a racing finish won; the function raises 55000 "already been finished", `20260907110000_finish_workout.sql:52`) → return `summaryFor`; otherwise throw the RPC's message (a real refusal, e.g. "Your program moved on…"); (iii) `finishWorkout` returns `await summaryFor(userId, workoutId)` and computes nothing of its own — one owner of "what this workout was". `summaryFor` reads the finished row + its sets with `readAllRows` (ordered `id`) and computes `durationMin/sets/volumeKg/volume/unit`; for `personalRecords`, `firstTimeLifts` and `changes` see Open question 4: **if yes (recommended)** — migration `supabase/migrations/20260917100000_workout_keeps_its_summary.sql`: `ALTER TABLE workout_logs ADD COLUMN progression_changes jsonb, ADD COLUMN personal_records jsonb` (both nullable; null = "not kept", never "none"), and `DROP FUNCTION finish_program_workout(UUID, TIMESTAMPTZ, INTEGER, SMALLINT, SMALLINT, TEXT, JSONB, JSONB, JSONB, INTEGER)` then `CREATE FUNCTION` with two more parameters `p_changes JSONB, p_records JSONB` written in the same `UPDATE workout_logs` as `ended_at` (same REVOKE/GRANT lines, SECURITY INVOKER, no policy change); `finishWorkout` computes `changes` (as today, `applyLog`) and the records (step 6's baseline restricted to workouts logged before this one, `{ records, firstTimeLifts }` or `null` when the baseline was unavailable) BEFORE the RPC and hands them in, so the receipt is exactly what the sheet said; `summaryFor` reads the two columns — `personal_records` null → `recordsUnavailable: true`, `progression_changes` null → `changesUnavailable: true, changes: []` (every workout finished before this migration reads that way, and the receipt says "what changed next time was not kept for this workout"; it never says "nothing changed"). **If no** — `summaryFor` recomputes records from step 6's baseline and `changes` by `replayEnrollment` over `getSessionLogs` logged before this workout, and returns `changesUnavailable: true` for an enrollment with no `initialExerciseState` (the case `recalculateEnrollment` refuses, `programRepo.ts:799–803`); the executor must know that this branch can show a receipt that differs from what the finish sheet said once the program is edited. `GET /api/workouts/[id]/summary` → the summary, 404 when `summaryFor` returns null (unknown, or still open). `finish/route.ts`: "no longer exists" → 404; every other thrown message → 409 as today. Change, client (`finish`): on `!res.ok` → `setError(body.error)` and keep the sheet — the server said no and said why (delete the guess; that path is a REFUSAL, not a lost reply); on a network error → `GET /api/workouts/live`: if it returns the same id → `setError("The reply was lost, and the workout is still open. Tap Save again.")` and keep the sheet; if it returns `null` or a different id → `clearStartKey(workout.enrollmentId)`, `setWorkout(null)`, then `GET /summary`: 200 → return it; 404 → return `null` and `setError("This workout was thrown away on another device.")` (the row is gone; nothing to show); 5xx/network → return `{ workoutId, unit, unavailable: true, personalRecords: [], firstTimeLifts: [], changes: [], durationMin: 0, sets: 0, volumeKg: 0, volume: 0 }`; if `/live` itself cannot be read → `setError("Could not reach the server to check whether it went through. Tap Save again when you have signal.")` and keep the sheet. Delete the sentence "Nothing was finished." `FinishSheet.tsx` summary branch: when `summary.unavailable` render `[data-testid=summary-unavailable]` amber "Saved, but the totals could not be worked out." with "—" for every number (spec S3 states table, never 0); when `summary.changesUnavailable` render "What changed for next time was not kept for this workout." in place of the "Next time" list. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/useLiveWorkout.test.tsx` — "a lost finish reply is checked against the server, and the summary is shown when it went through" (finish rejects, `/live` answers `null`, `/summary` answers a body → returned; `startKeyFor("e1")` is now a fresh key), "a lost finish reply that did not go through says so and keeps the workout open", "a finish the server refuses shows the server's sentence and never says the reply was lost" (409 `{ error: "A workout cannot end before it started." }` → that exact text, workout kept), "a workout thrown away elsewhere is reported as thrown away, not as saved" (`/live` null, `/summary` 404); `tests/unit/db/workoutRepoFinish.test.ts` — "finishing an already-finished workout returns its summary instead of an error", "a finish that loses the race to another finish still returns the summary" (RPC answers the 55000 error, the row re-read is closed), "the summary read afterwards is the same object finishWorkout returned" (same fixture through both paths; if Open question 4 is yes, assert the RPC was called with `p_changes`/`p_records` equal to what `summaryFor` then reads back — this is what makes `finishWorkout` "asserted" in `tests/support/writeCoverage.baseline.json`); `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` — "a dropped finish reply still ends in the summary, and Start works afterwards" (`page.route('**/finish', async r => { await r.fetch(); await r.abort() })` so the server processes it and the page never hears back; expect `workout-summary`; then `start-workout` → `/programs/live`). After a full page reload the summary has no screen until the receipt page `/programs/workout/[id]` exists — that is Phase 4's step (spec Decision 7); `summaryFor` is the function that page must render, so it is built here once.

5. **You cannot end, or push aside, a program that has a workout open on it.** `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `unenroll` (line 379), `enrollInProgram` (line 244, the displacement loop at 288–293), new `assertNoOpenWorkoutOn(userId, enrollmentId)` and new exported class `ProgramBusy extends Error`. Today: `unenroll` flips `is_active` with no check; `enrollInProgram` pauses same-discipline programs before inserting, with no check; the open workout then finishes onto a program nobody is shown any more and the new program sits at week 1 as if you had not trained. Change: `assertNoOpenWorkoutOn` runs one query on `workout_logs` (`user_id = ? and enrollment_id = ? and started_at is not null and ended_at is null`, `maybeSingle`) — its own query, not an import of `workoutRepo`, because `workoutRepo` already imports this file — and throws `ProgramBusy("Finish or throw away the open workout first.")`. `unenroll` calls it before the update; `enrollInProgram` calls it for every enrollment it is about to displace BEFORE the first write (so nothing is paused if it refuses); `startDraft` (`src/db/programDraftRepo.ts:218`) and `ensureEnrollment` (line 328) go through `enrollInProgram` and are covered. Routes: `app/api/programs/enrollments/[id]/route.ts` · `DELETE` and `app/api/programs/enrollments/route.ts` · `POST` return 409 with the message for `ProgramBusy` (the DELETE route already returns the message with 400; POST currently returns 500 — both become 409). Honest limit: this is a check, then a write — a Start that lands in the milliseconds between them still slips through; the database itself does not enforce it. Open question 5 offers the trigger that would (migration `supabase/migrations/20260917100100_program_busy_while_workout_open.sql`: `BEFORE UPDATE OF is_active ON program_enrollments`, when `OLD.is_active AND NOT NEW.is_active` and a live `workout_logs` row points at it → `RAISE EXCEPTION 'Finish or throw away the open workout first.'`; no policy change); if yes, `unenroll` and the displacement loop map that exception text to `ProgramBusy` so the client sees one sentence either way. What you will see until Phase 5: the End buttons on /programs (`ProgressionView.tsx:107`, `RunningPrograms.tsx:67`, `ProgramsApp.tsx:350`) ignore the reply today (programs-today-03, Phase 5's), so a refused End looks like nothing happened and the program stays — which is the correct outcome, silently; Life Mastery's Start already prints `body.error` (`WorkoutPrograms.tsx:202`), so the displacement refusal is visible there now, and its rebuild is Phase 8. Note for Phase 2: lm-templates-06 (insert-first, then deactivate) edits the same displacement loop; this guard sits before the loop and composes with that reorder. *Test:* `tests/unit/db/programRepoGuards.test.ts` — "ending a program with a workout open on it is refused", "starting a program that would displace one with a workout open is refused before anything is paused" (assert no `update({ is_active: false })` reached the fake), "ending a different program while a loose workout is open is allowed"; `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` — "End is refused while a workout is open on the program, by the API" (start; `DELETE /api/programs/enrollments/<id>` from `page.evaluate` → 409 and the sentence; the enrollment is still active).

6. **One definition of a personal best, all-time, stamped on the lifter's own day, and never on a first-ever set.** `src/health/healthService.ts` · `detectPersonalRecords` (line 232), `liftBests` (line 836) gains a required `timezone: string` parameter, new export `firstTimeLifts(prior, newSets): string[]`; `src/db/healthRepo.ts` · new exports `personalBestBaseline(userId, before?: { workoutId: string; loggedAt: string })` and `liftBestsAllTime(userId)`, `getExerciseMax` (line 600) and `createWorkoutLog` (line 194); `src/db/workoutRepo.ts` · delete `priorSetsFor`, `newRecords` and `PR_HISTORY_WORKOUTS` (lines 494–582); `src/programs/types.ts` · `WorkoutSummary` gains `firstTimeLifts: string[]`; `src/health/types.ts` · `WorkoutLogWithSets` callers unchanged; `app/api/health/workout/route.ts` · `POST` returns `personalRecords`, `firstTimeLifts` and `recordsUnavailable` on the 201 body — computed inside `createWorkoutLog` (it returns `{ ...log, sets, personalRecords, firstTimeLifts, recordsUnavailable }`), because the route is 46 code lines against a 50-line ceiling (`architecture.test.ts:106`); new route `app/api/health/workout/bests/route.ts` · `GET` → `liftBestsAllTime`; `src/programs/components/ProgressTab.tsx` · line 95 `liftBests(logs)` → a second fetch of `/api/health/workout/bests` (its own loading/failed/ready state; the failed state is the amber card with Try again, never an empty list; the file is already on the fetching allowlist so no entry is added); `src/health/components/WorkoutLogger.tsx` · lines 114 and 219–224: delete the client-side `detectPersonalRecords` call and read `personalRecords`/`firstTimeLifts` from the POST reply. Today: three windows — the finish summary's last 400 workouts (workoutRepo.ts:505), Progress's 365 days (ProgressTab.tsx:50), Anything-else's 90 days (WorkoutLogger.tsx:114) — so one lift is a record on one screen and not the next; with no history at all every set is a record (walk-05); and a FOURTH "best", `getExerciseMax` (the Squat 1RM tile), uses an uncapped Epley while `liftBests` caps the estimate at 10 reps (healthService.ts:848), so a 20-rep set inflates the tile and not the Progress list. Also `liftBests` stamps `bestWeightDate` with `localDateKey(new Date(logged_at))` (line 282–284, 840) — the browser's calendar today; moved server-side it would silently become the server's UTC calendar, the class of bug `.claude/rules/finished-work.md` names first. Change: `personalBestBaseline` reads every finished workout for the person with `readAllRows` (ordered `logged_at, id`; `finishedWorkouts` filter) and their sets in `chunkIds` batches — the same two-step paging `getWorkoutLogsWithSets` (healthRepo.ts:271–312) already uses, never a nested `workout_sets(*)` on an unpaged parent — optionally only workouts with `logged_at < before.loggedAt` and never `before.workoutId` itself, returning `{ sets, unavailable }` exactly as `priorSetsFor` did; `detectPersonalRecords` only claims a record for a lift that has at least one prior WORKING set — a lift with none goes to `firstTimeLifts`; `liftBests(logs, timezone)` uses `toDateISO(toZonedDate(new Date(log.logged_at), timezone))`; `liftBestsAllTime(userId)` = `liftBests(baseline as logs, await getUserTimezone(userId))`; `getExerciseMax` computes its estimate through `estimateOneRepMax` with the same ">10 reps → the weight itself" cap `liftBests` applies (extract `cappedEstimate(weightKg, reps)` in `healthService` and use it in both — one formula); every caller (finish via step 4, summary, past-workout POST, Progress bests, the 1RM tile) goes through these functions and nothing else computes a best. `FinishSheet.tsx` summary branch: "New best" only when `personalRecords.length > 0`; when `firstTimeLifts.length > 0`, one `text-sm text-muted-foreground` line "First time logged: Squat, Bench Press" (spec S3 element 24). Existing tests that assert the old behaviour and must change WITH this step: `tests/unit/health/healthService.test.ts:374–380` "still reports each lift separately" (Bench Press has no history there — it becomes a first-time lift; give the fixture a prior bench set), `tests/unit/health/progressReads.test.ts:111` (`bestWeightDate` — pass a timezone and assert the day in it), and `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts:189` "finishing shows what you did…" (the history is wiped, so the first workout's set is a FIRST, not a best: assert "First time logged" and no "New best" on this finish, then finish a second workout at a heavier weight in the same test and assert "New best" once, naming the lift). *Test:* `tests/unit/health/personalRecords.test.ts` — "no best is claimed for a lift with no history — it is a first", "a heavier set fourteen months ago beats today's, because the baseline is all time", "the finish summary, Progress bests and the past-workout form agree" (one fixture; `liftBests` top bench = 105, `detectPersonalRecords(prior, [102.5×5])` = none, `firstTimeLifts` = ["Front Squat"]), "a best is filed on the lifter's day, not the server's" (`liftBests` with `Europe/Copenhagen` for a 23:30 UTC set → the next calendar day); `tests/unit/health/personalBestBaseline.test.ts` — "reads every finished set, not the first thousand" (capped fake, 1,400 sets) and "excludes the workout being finished and anything logged after it"; `tests/unit/health/exerciseMax.test.ts` — "the 1RM tile and Your bests give the same estimate for the same set" (a 20-rep set: both say the weight itself); `tests/unit/health/workoutLogger.test.tsx` — "shows the record the server found, and computes none of its own"; `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` — "a lift never done before is 'first time logged', not a new best" (Add a lift "Front Squat", tick one set, finish: no "New best", text "First time logged"). The heading on Progress stays "Your bests" and is now true (Open question 7). Phase 7 reworks ProgressTab; it must keep reading `/api/health/workout/bests`. The unpaged-read counter in `architecture.test.ts` does not grow: both new reads are paged.

7. **A blank weight box is never saved as 0 kg on a lift that needs one; a lift you can do with nothing added still saves with nothing added.** New `src/shared/typedNumber.ts` · `typedNumber(s: string): number | null` (blank, whitespace or NaN → `null`); `src/programs/data/exerciseLibrary.ts` · `make()` (line 140) derives `unweightedOk: true` when the BEGINNER seed is 0, and new export `canBeUnweighted(libraryId?: string, name?: string): boolean` (by id, then `libraryByName`; unknown lift → false); `src/programs/types.ts` · `LibraryExercise` gains `unweightedOk?: true`; `src/programs/components/live/LiveWorkoutScreen.tsx` · the added-lift mapping (line 103) sets `unweightedOk: canBeUnweighted(a.libraryId, a.name)` and the prescribed mapping passes `canBeUnweighted(undefined, ex.name)`; `src/programs/components/live/SetRow.tsx` · lines 151–152, new prop `unweightedOk?: boolean`; `src/health/components/WorkoutLogger.tsx` · `flattenSets` (line 182). Why NOT "all three seeds are 0": Pull-up is seeded 0/5/20, Chin-up 0/5/20, Dip 0/10/25, Inverted Row 0/0/10 — the beginner does them unweighted and the advanced lifter adds plates. The "all zero" rule would file every one of them as weighted, and a beginner's pull-up with an empty box would be REFUSED — the exact pull-up case after-the-fact-logging-06 reports. The fact "this movement can be done with nothing added" is already in the data as a zero beginner seed; the flag is derived from it in one place so the two cannot drift. Today: the ✓ is disabled only while reps are blank and `Number("")` turns an empty weight into 0 (line 152); the server accepts 0 because 0 is legitimate bodyweight work, so nothing downstream can tell "unweighted" from "forgot to type". Change: on a row where `!bodyweight && !unweightedOk`, the ✓ is disabled while `typedNumber(weight) === null` or `typedNumber(reps) === null`; on an `unweightedOk` row the box stays (weighted pull-ups exist), its placeholder reads "+kg" / "+lb" (`unitLabel`), and blank sends 0 — that IS the rule "nothing added"; a `bodyweight` row (skill/hold programs, `programsService.ts:1080,1134`) keeps hiding the box and sends 0, unchanged. The weight `Input` gets `min={0} max={MAX_WEIGHT_KG} step="any"` (the same number in whichever unit the box shows — `CompleteSetSchema` bounds the typed number before conversion, so this is never looser than the server), reps `min={0} max={1000} step={1}`; a value outside those bounds is refused on the row with a sentence naming the limit, so the server's 400 is never the first check (pairs with step 8). `WorkoutLogger.flattenSets`: blank weight with reps typed on a lift `canBeUnweighted(undefined, name)` says is unweighted-ok → 0; blank weight with reps typed on any other lift → the save is refused with a visible message naming the lift; blank reps with a weight → refused; a "weights" session that would post no sets → refused (today `sets: undefined` lets an empty gym session through). *Test:* `tests/unit/shared/typedNumber.test.ts` — "blank is nothing, not zero"; `tests/unit/programs/setRow.test.tsx` — "will not tick a barbell lift with the weight box empty", "ticks a pull-up with the weight box empty, sending 0" and "ticks a plank row that has no weight box, sending 0"; `tests/unit/programs/lifts.test.ts` — "a lift seeded at 0 for beginners can be done unweighted, and only those" (asserts `unweightedOk` ⇔ `suggestedKg.beginner === 0` over the whole library, and names Pull-up, Dip, Push-up as true and Bench Press, Assisted Pull-up as false); `tests/unit/health/workoutLogger.test.tsx` — "keeps a pull-up set with no weight, and refuses a bench set with none"; `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — "no NEW form turns a blank box into a number" (grep `Number\((weight|reps|set\.weight|set\.reps)\)` under `src/programs/components` and `src/health/components`; allowlist after this phase = `HistoryTab.tsx` (Phase 7, history-progress-07) and `CustomProgramBuilder.tsx` (Phase 8, its use is guarded by a trim check), and "the blank-to-number allowlist only shrinks"). If Decision 3 of the design spec deletes WorkoutLogger in Phase 4, its edit here goes with it; the helper, the library flag and the SetRow rule stay. Phase 6 rebuilds SetRow's markup and keeps these props. Open question 2 records the one place this departs from the spec's wording.

8. **A set the server refuses comes off the screen now, says why, and the rest clock it started is cleared.** `src/programs/hooks/useLiveWorkout.ts` · `tick` (line 222; the throw at 264 and the catch at 267–271); `src/programs/components/live/LiveWorkoutScreen.tsx` · `onTick` (line 381); `app/api/workouts/[id]/sets/route.ts` · `POST` (line 18). Today: every non-OK reply is thrown and queued as if offline, so a 400 sits ticked and "waiting for signal" for up to 20 s until `flush` drops it; the rest clock has already started; the route's 400 body is the bare "Could not save that set". Change: `tick` returns `Promise<TickOutcome>` with `TickOutcome = "saved" | "queued" | "refused" | "no-workout"`; a 4xx reply removes the optimistic set immediately, sets `error` to `"That ${exercise} set could not be saved: ${body.error}"` and does not queue; a network error or 5xx queues as today; `LiveWorkoutScreen.onTick` starts the rest clock at once, as today (a clock that waits for the round trip starts late on gym wifi and, offline, not until the request gives up — the training project's offline spec would regress), and when the awaited result is `"refused"` it clears it (`setRestFrom(null)`); the sets route returns the first zod issue message like the finish route does (`parsed.error.issues[0]?.message`). *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/useLiveWorkout.test.tsx` — "a set the server refuses comes off the screen at once and is never queued" (400 → `unsaved` stays 0, `workout.sets` empty, `error` names the lift, result `"refused"`), "a set that cannot reach the server is queued and the result says so" (`"queued"`); `tests/unit/programs/liveWorkoutScreen.test.tsx` — "the rest clock starts on the tick and is cleared when the server refuses the set" (render with a deferred 400 fetch; after the tick `rest-bar` is present; after the reply resolves it is gone) and "the rest clock stays when the set is merely queued".

9. **A lift you added on the day is never listed as a miss.** `src/programs/programsService.ts` · new export `unfinishedLifts(prescribed: PrescribedExercise[], adjustments: WorkoutAdjustments, sets: LiveWorkoutSet[]): { short: { exerciseId; name; done; asked }[]; untouchedAdded: { exerciseId; name }[] }`; `src/programs/components/live/LiveWorkoutScreen.tsx` · the inline `unfinished` computation (lines 131–139); `src/programs/components/live/FinishSheet.tsx` · the `unfinished` prop (line 30) and the amber list (lines 159–197). Today: `unfinished` runs over `exercises`, which includes added lifts with three placeholder rows, so "Front Squat 0 of 3" is presented as a miss (walk §4). Change: `short` covers prescribed lifts not in `adjustments.skipped` with `asked > 0` only (warm-ups excluded from `done`, as today); lifts in `adjustments.added` with no ticked set are returned separately as `untouchedAdded`; the sheet lists them under the amber box as "Front Squat — added, nothing ticked" with no "of N" and no miss wording. The screen calls the pure function and nothing else computes this list. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/unfinishedLifts.test.ts` — "a lift added on the day is never a miss", "a prescribed lift with two of five sets is short by three", "a skipped lift is not listed", "an added lift with one ticked set is listed nowhere"; `tests/unit/programs/liveWorkoutScreen.test.tsx` — "a lift added on the day is listed as added, never as '0 of 3'". Phase 6 adds the "drop it" affordance on that line (spec S3 element 22) and the per-lift miss-count wording (live-workout-06).

10. **A live run is stored as a run and a live stretch as mobility, decided in one place — and no database file carries a session-type literal.** `src/programs/programsService.ts` · new export `sessionTypeFor(program: ProgramDefinition | null): "weights" | "cardio" | "running" | "mobility"` (moved from `src/db/programRepo.ts:901`, which imports it back for `writeWorkout` line 870; `null` — a workout on no program — answers `BRIDGE_SESSION_TYPE`, i.e. "weights", until Phase 6 gives a loose workout a way to say otherwise); `src/db/workoutRepo.ts` · `startWorkout` line 161 and `finishWorkout` lines 408–414; `src/programs/schemas.ts` · `FinishWorkoutSchema` (line 317, the `sessionType` field). Today: `startWorkout` hard-codes `session_type: "weights"`; `finishWorkout` has a follow-up `update({ session_type })` after the transaction whose error is discarded and which no caller sends (`sessionType` on the schema is dead), so every live run counts as a gym session and the running tiles never move. Change: `startWorkout` writes `session_type: sessionTypeFor(program)`; delete the follow-up update and the `sessionType` field from `FinishWorkoutSchema` and `finishWorkout`'s input — the silent fallback goes, not gets fixed. *Test:* `tests/unit/db/workoutRepoStart.test.ts` — "a live workout on a running program is stored as a run, on a mobility program as mobility, on StrongLifts as weights, on no program as weights"; `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — "only sessionTypeFor decides what a workout counts as" (grep `session_type:\s*"` under `src/db` — zero matches; the interface line `session_type: string` and `session_type: sessionTypeFor(program)` do not match by construction); `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` — "a run started from its program is a run in the database" (enrol `couch-to-5k` via the API, Start from `today-card`, the live page opens with `[data-testid=endurance-plan]` — this is the assertion step 1's `prescriptionForDay` fix exists for — Finish, read `/api/health/workout?days=1` → `session_type === "running"`). The integration schema `tests/integration/schema.sql` still lacks `finish_program_workout` (data-integrity-07, Phase 10); nothing in this step changes SQL.

11. **Keep the guards honest.** `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` gains the three tests named above (steps 3, 7, 10) and loses two entries from `COMPONENTS_THAT_FETCH_THEIR_OWN_DATA` (step 3). Write functions: `summaryFor`, `assertNoOpenWorkoutOn`, `personalBestBaseline`, `liftBestsAllTime` only read, so they do not appear in `tests/support/writeCoverage.baseline.json`; `startWorkout` and `finishWorkout` become "asserted" (the fake-client tests assert on the inserted payload and the RPC arguments) and `createWorkoutLog` may move from "mentioned" to "asserted" — the unasserted ceiling (`MAX_UNASSERTED`, 136) is never raised. New API routes (`/api/workouts/[id]/summary`, `/api/health/workout/bests`) are not pages (`routeReachability` skips `app/api`, line 88), so `routeReachability` and `backNavigation` are unaffected; run them anyway.
