Migrations: none unless Open questions 4 or 5 are yes; if either is, the executor runs `supabase migration list --linked`, confirms nothing is pending that is not theirs, writes the file(s) named in steps 4 and 5, and pushes; `tests/integration/schema.sql` is not touched (data-integrity-07 is Phase 10). *Test:* `npm test` green with no entry added to `.test-known-failures.json`; `npx playwright test --project=setup --project=training tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` green.

### Acceptance
- `tests/unit/db/workoutRepoStart.test.ts` — "starts the day the card named, not the day the cursor points at" proves the server's default day is the account-weekday pick, with the timezone read from the profile and the clock injected; "an unanchored program still starts the cursor's day" proves StrongLifts did not change; "a Couch to 5K session opens its prescription without asking for days" proves an endurance live screen no longer crashes; "a key whose workout is already finished is refused as spent…", "a key on a workout that was written after the fact is spent too…", "a lost reply retried with the same key returns the workout it opened", "two starts racing both end on the one workout, without a Postgres message" and "a day that is not in the program is refused by name" prove no path from `startWorkout` can throw Postgres text and that "open" means started-and-not-ended, nothing looser; "a live workout on a running program is stored as a run…" proves the inserted `session_type` comes from one function.
- `tests/unit/programs/engine.test.ts` — "isSessionOf knows an endurance session id and refuses a made-up one" proves the day check has one owner across every schedule kind.
- `tests/unit/programs/trainingCardState.test.ts` — "carries the day id so Start opens the day it named" proves the card state cannot name a day without carrying its id.
- `tests/unit/programs/useLiveWorkout.test.tsx` — the five start-key tests prove the key lives only between "sent" and "any reply", rotates once when spent, survives a lost reply and a server error; the four finish tests prove the hook asks the server only when it heard nothing, repeats the server's own sentence when it heard a refusal, and never calls a thrown-away workout "saved"; the two tick tests prove a 400 is not treated as offline and a network failure still is.
- `tests/unit/db/workoutRepoFinish.test.ts` — "finishing an already-finished workout returns its summary instead of an error" and "a finish that loses the race to another finish still returns the summary" prove Save is idempotent in both orders; "the summary read afterwards is the same object finishWorkout returned" proves `summaryFor` is the one owner of "what this workout was".
- `tests/unit/db/programRepoGuards.test.ts` — three tests prove End and Start-another refuse before any write when a workout is open on the program, and do not refuse for an unrelated one.
- `tests/unit/health/personalRecords.test.ts`, `tests/unit/health/personalBestBaseline.test.ts`, `tests/unit/health/exerciseMax.test.ts` — prove one all-time, paged baseline; no record on a first-ever lift; a best filed on the lifter's own day; four callers (finish, Progress, past-workout form, 1RM tile) agree on one fixture and one formula. `tests/unit/health/workoutLogger.test.tsx` proves the past-workout form computes no best of its own.
- `tests/unit/shared/typedNumber.test.ts`, `tests/unit/programs/setRow.test.tsx`, `tests/unit/programs/lifts.test.ts`, `tests/unit/health/workoutLogger.test.tsx` — prove blank is never 0 on a lift that needs a weight, IS 0 on a lift you can do unweighted, that the library decides which is which in one derived flag, and that both forms apply the same rule.
- `tests/unit/programs/unfinishedLifts.test.ts` and `tests/unit/programs/liveWorkoutScreen.test.tsx` — prove an added lift is never a miss and a refused set clears the rest clock it started.
- `tests/e2e/programs-live-workout.spec.ts` (training project, one worker, 390×844) — seven new tests: the racing starts, the two-device finish, the dropped finish reply, the API-level End refusal, the run that is a run, the first-time lift, and the rewritten "finishing shows what you did" that now finishes twice to see a first and then a best. Each is a flow a person would notice, run through the real routes against the real database.
- Guards that must stay green, with allowlists that may shrink and never grow: `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` (including its three new tests and the unpaged-read, UTC-date, fetching-screens and small-text ratchets — the fetching-screens allowlist loses `TodayCard.tsx` and `StartLooseWorkout.tsx`), `tests/unit/navigation/routeReachability.test.ts`, `tests/unit/navigation/backNavigation.test.ts`, `tests/unit/writeCoverage.test.ts` (the unasserted ceiling is not raised). Baseline measured by the attacker on 2026-09-17: all seven guard/hook files pass (92/92).
- Each step is done only when its named test passes AND fails when the step's change is reverted — the executor reverts once per step to check.

### Attack log
- Step 1 would have crashed every endurance start that carries a day id: `scheduleDays()` throws for `endurance_weeks` (`src/programs/customize.ts:123–126`), and TodayCard always sends `dayId`. Worse, `prescriptionForDay` (`workoutRepo.ts:623`) already calls it unconditionally, so `/programs/live` cannot open for a Couch to 5K workout today — the critic finding describing that screen was never run in a browser. Fixed: `isSessionOf(program, dayId)` in the engine handles both shapes; `prescriptionForDay` skips `scheduleDays` for endurance; two tests added; step 10's e2e now asserts the endurance plan card renders.
- Step 2(a) treated "a `client_key` row with `ended_at` null" as the open workout. The after-the-fact writer stores `client_key` on rows with `started_at` null (`programRepo.ts:869–883`); `toLive()` dereferences `started_at!`. Rewritten: a key is a retry only when it names the LIVE workout; any other row with that key is spent. Test added for the never-live row.
- Step 2 answered a sequential second tap with 409 `already_open` and a racing second tap with a 201 carrying the winner's workout — two outcomes for one situation, two client paths. Unified: both are `already_open` carrying the workout; the client has one rule.
- Step 3 removed the only `fetch(` from `TodayCard.tsx` and `StartLooseWorkout.tsx` but not their entries in `COMPONENTS_THAT_FETCH_THEIR_OWN_DATA` (`architecture.test.ts:653–654`); "the fetching-screens allowlist only shrinks" would fail on the first run. Named the removal.
- Step 4 told the person "The reply was lost" on any non-OK finish while the workout was still open — but every finish error is a 409 today (`finish/route.ts:23`), including "A workout cannot end before it started.", which is a refusal, not a lost reply. Split by evidence: a body with `error` is a refusal and is shown verbatim; only a network failure triggers the `/live` check. Test added.
- Step 4 left a finish that loses a race to another finish as an on-screen error for a workout that is in fact closed (`finish_program_workout` raises 55000, `20260907110000:52`). Server now re-reads the row after any RPC error and returns the summary when it is closed. Test added.
- Step 4's fallback said "Saved. The totals could not be loaded" when `/live` was null and `/summary` failed — which is also what a workout DISCARDED on another device looks like. A 404 now says "thrown away on another device"; only 5xx/network is "unavailable". Test added.
- Step 4's copy and testid for the unavailable summary diverged from spec S3 (`[data-testid=summary-unavailable]`, "Saved, but the totals could not be worked out."). Adopted the spec's.
- Step 4 recomputed `changes` by replay for the receipt: it drifts from what the sheet said once the program is edited, and it cannot run at all for an enrollment without `initialExerciseState` (`recalculateEnrollment` refuses those, `programRepo.ts:799–803`) — "the summary read afterwards equals the one returned at finish" would be false by design. Replaced with storing `progression_changes` and `personal_records` inside the finish transaction (one migration, Open question 4, recommended), replay kept as the described alternative; `changesUnavailable` added so an old workout never reads "nothing changed".
- Step 6 moved `liftBests` server-side without noticing it stamps dates with `localDateKey(new Date(...))` (`healthService.ts:282–284, 840`) — the browser's calendar today, the SERVER's (UTC) calendar tomorrow: the "whose clock" class named first in finished-work.md. `liftBests` now takes the account timezone; `progressReads.test.ts:111` named for the change.
- Step 6 did not name the two existing tests that assert the behaviour it removes: `healthService.test.ts:374–380` expects a lift with no history to be a record, and `programs-live-workout.spec.ts:189` expects "new best" on a wiped account's first set — exactly walk-05. Both rewritten in the step; the e2e now finishes twice to see a first and then a best.
- Step 6 named three definitions of a best; there are four. `getExerciseMax` (`healthRepo.ts:600–649`, the Squat 1RM tile) uses an uncapped Epley while `liftBests` caps at 10 reps. Folded into one shared estimate with a test.
- Step 6 put the record computation in `app/api/health/workout/route.ts`, which is at 46 code lines against the 50-line ceiling (`architecture.test.ts:106`, counted with the test's own rule). Moved into `createWorkoutLog`'s return.
- Step 7's bodyweight rule ("all three seed weights are 0") misfiles Pull-up (0/5/20), Chin-up (0/5/20), Dip (0/10/25) and Inverted Row (0/0/10) as weighted — a beginner pull-up with an empty box would be refused, the exact case after-the-fact-logging-06 reports, and the draft's `carried()` change would have hidden the box for weighted push-ups. Rule replaced: beginner seed 0 ⇔ can be done unweighted, derived in `make()` so the flag and the seed cannot disagree; the box stays; blank = 0 only there. Test asserts the equivalence over the whole library.
- Step 8 awaited the tick before starting the rest clock. That delays the clock by a round trip and, offline, until the request gives up — a regression of the offline flow the training project tests. Clock now starts on the tick and is cleared on a refusal; tests reworded.
- Step 10's architecture grep (`session_type:` under `src/db`) would flag `session_type: sessionTypeFor(program)` (`programRepo.ts:870`) and the interface line `session_type: string` (`workoutRepo.ts:58`), and its own ternary kept a literal. `sessionTypeFor(null)` now owns the loose-workout answer and the test forbids `session_type:\s*"` under `src/db` outright.
- Step 5 called a read-then-write a "guard". It is a check with a window; said so, and offered the trigger that closes it as Open question 5.
- Step 5's refusal is invisible on /programs until Phase 5 because three End handlers ignore the reply (`ProgressionView.tsx:107`, `RunningPrograms.tsx:67`, `ProgramsApp.tsx:350`). Stated in plain language in the step rather than left for the owner to discover; the e2e for this step asserts at the API.
- Blockers paragraph was stale: `20260914120000_drop_four_orphan_tables.sql` is committed and applied (`supabase migration list --linked`, 2026-09-17), so a migration from this phase is pushable if the list is still clean at execution. Rewritten with the precondition.
- Line drift corrected: `TrainingCardState` is at `programsService.ts:1920` (draft: 1916); `tick`'s throw/catch at 264/267–271; the fake-client baseline is 92/92 across seven files, not 88/88.
- Step 1 left the rest-day default unstated (no `dayId`, weekday-pinned rest day → `todaysSessionFor` returns the NEXT session flagged `restDay`). Stated: starting it is the spec's "Start Workout B early", not an error.
- Two places where a step departs from the agreed spec were picked silently: step 7 vs S3 element 9 ("disabled until reps typed") and step 3's error sentence vs S1 element 8's fixed "Could not start that workout." Both raised as Open questions 2 and 3 with recommendations.
- Checked and found sound: every other named file and symbol exists at the cited line (`getTodaySession` 590, `startWorkout` 120, `finishWorkout` 326, `finish` 334, `startKeyFor` 423, `clearStartKey` 440, `unenroll` 379, `enrollInProgram` 244 with the loop at 288–293, `detectPersonalRecords` 232, `liftBests` 836, `priorSetsFor` 520, `PR_HISTORY_WORKOUTS` 505, `sessionTypeFor` 901, `writeWorkout` 870, `WorkoutSummary` 749, `SetRow.tsx` 151–152, `flattenSets` 182, `unfinished` 131–139, `carried` 560, `replayEnrollment` 1815, `isoWeekdayInTimezone` 102, `getUserTimezone` 153); no step writes an RLS policy or touches auth; nothing destructive is ungated (the only migrations are additive and gated on Open questions 4 and 5); no new icon is introduced; every e2e test lives in `programs-live-workout.spec.ts`, which is already in the `training` project.

### Open questions for Phase 0

Numbered as the steps above refer to them. Each carries a recommendation.

**Open question 1.** When you finish a run or a stretch that you started from the live screen, should the finish sheet ask how far you went (a distance box, in km), in addition to how long it took? The audit says a run without a distance is just a timer; the agreed screen design for the live workout (S3) does not have a distance box. This phase only makes sure the session is stored as a run instead of a gym session (and fixes the crash that stopped a live run from opening at all); the box itself would be built in Phase 6 and needs one small database change (adding a distance parameter to the finish function).

*Recommendation:* Yes — add the distance box for endurance sessions in Phase 6, with the one migration that goes with it. Without it, a live run records nothing a runner cares about.

**Open question 2.** On a barbell or dumbbell lift, should the ✓ stay greyed out while the weight box is empty? The agreed design (S3, element 9) says the ✓ is disabled 'until reps typed' and says nothing about the weight; the audit found that ticking with the weight empty silently saves the set as 0 kg. This phase greys the ✓ out until BOTH boxes have a number — except on lifts you can do with nothing added (pull-ups, dips, push-ups, planks), where an empty box honestly means 'nothing added' and saves as 0.

*Recommendation:* Grey it out. A set saved as 0 kg on a bench press is wrong data that then hides in every total and every best; the pull-up case keeps working because the library knows which lifts can be done unweighted.

**Open question 3.** When Start fails on the Tracking card, which sentence should the card show? The agreed design (S1, element 8) uses one fixed line, 'Could not start that workout.' plus an 'Open training' link. The shared start helper built here returns a more specific sentence depending on what happened — for a lost connection it says 'Could not reach the server. Tap Start again — if it did go through, this opens that same workout.', which is the wording the /programs card already uses and the audit asked the Tracking card to match.

*Recommendation:* Show the helper's sentence, keep the spec's 'Open training' link. The specific sentence tells you what to do; the fixed one does not.

**Open question 4.** Should the app keep, on each finished workout, what it told you at the time — the 'New best' lines and the 'Next time: Squat +2.5 kg' changes — so the receipt page and History can show exactly that later? Keeping it means one small database change (two extra columns on the workout row, written in the same transaction as the finish). Not keeping it means recomputing every time you open a receipt, which can say something different once you have edited the program, and cannot be done at all for programs started before June (their starting weights were not kept).

*Recommendation:* Keep it — the receipt should be a record of what happened, and the recompute path has cases where it cannot answer. One migration, applied by the executor after checking nothing else is pending.

**Open question 5.** Should the database itself refuse to end or pause a program while a workout is open on it (a trigger — a rule the database enforces), or is the check in the app's code enough? The code check leaves a gap of a few milliseconds where a Start and an End can cross; the trigger closes it for everyone, including scripts. It is one more small migration and no change to who can see or edit what.

*Recommendation:* Yes, add the trigger in the same push as question 4's migration. The rule 'if it must hold for everyone it is a constraint or a trigger' is already in the project's database rules.

**Open question 6.** When two Starts race (two tabs, or the Tracking card and the Training page tapped within a second), the loser of the race is now told a workout is already open and is taken straight to it, rather than being shown an error. Is that what you want, or should the second tap be refused with a message?

*Recommendation:* Open the workout. The tap meant 'get me into a workout', and one is open; an error would send the person to look for a problem that does not exist.

**Open question 7.** 'New best' and 'Your bests' now mean your best ever, not your best this year. A lifter coming back from an injury may go months without seeing 'New best' because an old, heavier set stands. Keep it all-time (the Progress heading already says 'Your bests' with no period), or change the heading to 'Your bests this year' and keep the yearly window?

*Recommendation:* All-time. The alternative is a screen that calls a lighter lift a record; the finish sheet still says 'First time logged' for a new lift, so there is something to celebrate on the first session back.


### Blockers for Phase 0

No blockers.


## Phase 1 — The program engine tells the truth about weights, days and time

**What you can do after:** Nothing looks different yet — this phase is underneath the screens. What changes is that every button that touches your program now does exactly what its words say, or tells you why it did not: "End program" cannot claim to have ended a program that is still running, "Reset to start" says truthfully what happens to your weights, "Skip" is only offered where skipping means something and asks first, deleting or correcting a past session either moves the weights *and* removes the session or does neither, and starting a program can never switch your old one off and then fail. The week strip and the session card name the same day, worked out on your own clock; a brand-new account gets its timezone from the phone it signs up on instead of silently running on UTC (the clock that belongs to no country) until someone finds Settings; and a program with more than a thousand sessions still recalculates from all of them.

**Why it is in this order:** Phases 3, 5 and 8 build new screens on top of "what is today", "which program is running" and "what did that button do"; if the engine underneath lies about any of those, the new screens inherit the lie. Phase 0 (start/finish) is the only thing before this because a workout that cannot be finished blocks every test below.

**Findings this phase closes:** critic-reset-says-weights-go-back-but-keeps-them, data-integrity-03, history-progress-05, after-the-fact-logging-02, after-the-fact-logging-11, programs-today-03, programs-today-08, programs-today-07, data-integrity-08, critic-today-is-utc-until-you-find-settings, lm-templates-06, critic-end-or-displace-program-while-workout-open

### Plain-language summary of the six faults and the one rule that fixes each

| What goes wrong today | The one rule after this phase |
|---|---|
| Deleting a session on a program you edited after starting: the session is gone, the weights are not recalculated, the screen says "could not be deleted". | **Every write that moves the program is one database statement.** The new weights are worked out *before* anything is written; then the session removal (or the corrected sets, or the new program, or the ended program) and the new weights are committed together, or not at all. Same pattern as the existing `finish_program_workout`. |
| Replay walks old sessions against today's schedule and crashes on a lift you added later. | **The replay history records every schedule the program has had** — the one you started with and every edit — so each session replays against the schedule that was in force when it was logged. |
| "End program" / "Run it again" / "End it — I am done" never look at the server's answer. | **One client module owns every program action** and returns the server's sentence; a test forbids any other file from calling those routes. |
| Strip says Wednesday, card says Tuesday. | **The server hands down today's weekday and this week's trained days**, computed on the account's clock; a test forbids the training screens from reading the browser clock at all. |
| A new account's "today" is UTC until Settings is opened. | **The app knows whether the zone is known.** Signup records the phone's zone; a small one-time sync sets it for existing accounts that still hold the signup default; every prescription carries `clockKnown`. |
| Replay reads at most 1,000 sessions and says nothing. | **Every history read is paged**; the architecture allowance for unpaged reads goes down, never up. |

### Steps

1. **Program writes become one statement each (migration).** `supabase/migrations/20260917100000_program_writes_are_one_statement.sql` (new) · six `SECURITY INVOKER` functions, same shape as `finish_program_workout` (`supabase/migrations/20260907110000_finish_workout.sql`): an inner `assert_no_open_workout_on(p_ids uuid[])`, and the five callable ones `end_enrollment(p_id uuid)`, `start_enrollment(p_row jsonb, p_displace uuid[])`, `resume_enrollment(p_id uuid, p_displace uuid[])`, `remove_session_and_replay(p_log_id uuid, p_enrollment_id uuid, p_exercise_state jsonb, p_cursor jsonb, p_replay_events jsonb, p_expected_session_count int)`, `replace_sets_and_replay(p_log_id uuid, p_sets jsonb, p_enrollment_id uuid, p_exercise_state jsonb, p_cursor jsonb, p_replay_events jsonb, p_expected_session_count int)`, `log_session_and_advance(p_workout jsonb, p_sets jsonb, p_exercise_state jsonb, p_cursor jsonb, p_expected_session_count int)`. Today: `enrollInProgram` deactivates every same-discipline program *then* inserts (`src/db/programRepo.ts:287-316`, no transaction); `resumeEnrollment` does the same two-step (`:450-468`); `unenroll` flips `is_active` with no check for an open workout (`:379-387`); `reviseSessionLog` deletes the workout then replays (`:735-766`); `reviseWorkout` deletes sets, re-inserts, restores by hand on failure, then replays (`src/db/workoutRepo.ts:719-762`); `writeWorkout` inserts the workout, then the sets, then `persistState` runs — three writes, no rollback (`programRepo.ts:849-891`, `:706-707`). Change: (a) `end_enrollment`, `start_enrollment` and `resume_enrollment` all call `assert_no_open_workout_on` with the ids being ended or displaced — if a `workout_logs` row with `started_at is not null and ended_at is null` points at any of them, `RAISE EXCEPTION 'Finish or throw away the workout you have open first' USING ERRCODE = '55000'`. `start_enrollment` inserts via `jsonb_populate_record(null::program_enrollments, p_row)` **before** deactivating `p_displace`, and `resume_enrollment` sets its row active before deactivating, so a refused insert (the partial unique index `uq_program_enrollments_active`, a constraint) leaves the old program untouched. What is *not* closed by this: two tabs starting two *different* strength programs at the same moment can both end up running — the database has no rule for "one per discipline" because the discipline lives in the catalogue in code. That state is representable today and stays representable; it is named here so nobody reads "one statement" as "no race". (b) The two `*_and_replay` functions lock the enrollment `FOR UPDATE`, refuse with `'Your program moved on while this was being recalculated — reload and try again'` (55000) when `(cursor->>'sessionCount')::int <> p_expected_session_count`, then delete the workout row / delete-and-reinsert the sets via `jsonb_populate_recordset(null::workout_sets, p_sets)` and update `exercise_state, cursor, replay_events` in the same transaction. (c) `log_session_and_advance` inserts the workout `ON CONFLICT (user_id, client_key) WHERE client_key IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING` (the partial index is `uq_workout_logs_client_key`, `20260907100000_one_workout_record.sql:115`); when nothing was inserted it returns `{ workout_id, inserted: false }` and **does not** touch the enrollment (a retry is a no-op, as the column comment at `:352` already promises); otherwise it inserts the sets and advances the enrollment with the same session-count guard, returning `{ workout_id, inserted: true }`. (d) `REVOKE ALL … FROM PUBLIC; GRANT EXECUTE … TO authenticated` for each of the five callable functions (the inner assert is not granted) — **ASK FIRST, Open question 1** (it is a permission grant; the precedent is `finish_program_workout`, and no RLS *policy* changes). (e) The same functions are added to `tests/integration/schema.sql` under a dated changelog line at the top (the file says "keep in sync"; the `authenticated` role exists there, `schema.sql:594`, so the GRANT lines copy verbatim; `finish_program_workout` is also missing there — that is data-integrity-07, Phase 10's, and is left to it). Apply with `supabase migration list --linked` first; the untracked `20260914120000_drop_four_orphan_tables.sql` is another agent's and is already applied (walk §7), so `db push` must show only this phase's two files as pending. *Test:* `tests/integration/db/programRepo.integration.test.ts` (calls the functions through `getClient().query("select …")`, the way that file already tests constraints — the function *is* the production code here) — "ending a program with a workout open on it is refused, and the program stays running"; "a start whose insert is refused leaves the program you were on running"; "removing a session and moving the weights is one statement — a stale session count is refused and the session stays"; "the same write-up sent twice records one workout and advances the program once".

2. **The replay history records every schedule the program has had.** `src/programs/types.ts` · `ReplayEvent` (L460) gains `{ at: string; kind: "schedule"; schedule: ProgramSchedule | null; seeded: Record<string, ExerciseState> }`. Three writers, one rule: (i) `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `enrollInProgram` — today writes `custom_schedule: input.customSchedule ?? null` (L310) and no event, so a program started *with* its own schedule (every self-built week: its catalogue entry `custom` is a single placeholder day, `src/programs/data/customProgram.ts:28-44`) has no record of what it started as. Change: when `input.customSchedule` is non-null, the row handed to `start_enrollment` carries `replay_events: [{ at: <now>, kind: "schedule", schedule: input.customSchedule, seeded: {} }]`. (ii) · `updateEnrollmentSchedule` — today appends only `weight` events, and only when `changed.length > 0` (L546-560); the lifts `seedForAddedExercises` creates are written into `exercise_state` and nowhere else, so `initial_exercise_state` never learns about them. Change: always append one `schedule` event carrying the new schedule (null when restoring the catalogue) and exactly the state entries `seedForAddedExercises` created (the keys in its output that were not in `enr.exerciseState`), **before** any `weight` events from the same save (they share one `at`; `Array.prototype.sort` is stable, so array order decides ties — say so in a comment). (iii) `src/programs/programsService.ts` · `replayEnrollment` (L1815) — **signature unchanged** (`engine.test.ts` calls it twelve times as `(program, seed, logs)` and those stay green); what changes is meaning: `program` is the **catalogue** program, and when the timeline reaches a `schedule` event the walk switches to `effectiveProgram(catalog, event.schedule)`, merges `event.seeded` into state for lifts that have none, and clamps `cursor.dayIndex` with `clampCursorDay` (`src/programs/customize.ts:596`) — without the clamp a session logged on a day that a later edit removed falls back to the cursor's index (`applyLog`, `programsService.ts:605-606`) and `dayAt` throws past the end of the shorter list. `recalculateEnrollment`'s successor (step 3) passes `requireProgram(enr.program_id)` instead of `programFor(enr)`. Legacy rows: a new pure `repairUneventfulEdit(enr): ReplayEvent | null` in `programsService.ts` — when `customSchedule` is non-null and no `schedule` event exists, synthesise one dated `started_at` whose `schedule` is the **current** `customSchedule` and whose `seeded` is the current `exerciseState` entry for each lift missing from `initialExerciseState` (with `consecutiveFails: 0`); the replay folds it in and it is persisted with the next replay (the executor first runs the read-only probe `begin; select count(*) from program_enrollments where custom_schedule is not null and not (replay_events @> '[{"kind":"schedule"}]'); rollback;` and records the number in the attack log — the repair exists whatever the number is). Honest limit: for those legacy rows every session replays against today's schedule, because the schedule they were logged under was never kept — the crash goes away, the day-index fallback for a removed day does not, and cannot. Update the `replay_events` column comment in the step-1 migration. `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — new test "custom_schedule is written in one place" (`custom_schedule:` inside an `.update(`/`.insert(`/rpc payload appears only in `src/db/programRepo.ts`; allowlist that one file, may only shrink). *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/enginePhase0.test.ts`, describe "rebuilding the weights" — "a lift added after the start keeps its starting weight when an earlier session is removed" (enrol → add Face Pull @20 → log two → replay without the first → no throw, Face Pull still 20); "a session logged before a day was removed still moves the lifts that day had, and the cursor lands on a day that exists"; "a self-built week replays against the week that was built, not the catalogue's placeholder"; "an edit made before edits were recorded is replayed from the weights it had, not from nothing".

3. **Delete and correct compute first, commit once.** `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `recalculateEnrollment` (L770-810: reads, replays, `persistState` — and is called *after* the delete at `healthRepo.ts:435` and after the set rewrite at `workoutRepo.ts:762`). Change: split into `replayedState(userId, enrollmentId, change?)` — reads seed, logs and events, applies `change` (`{ withoutLogId }` or `{ replaceLogId, entries }`) to the log list in memory, returns `{ enrollment, expectedSessionCount }` and **writes nothing** — and `removeProgramSession(userId, enrollmentId, logId)` which calls `replayedState({ withoutLogId })` and then the single rpc `remove_session_and_replay`. `reviseSessionLog` (L735) becomes that call (its DELETE route is unchanged in shape). `src/db/healthRepo.ts` · `deleteWorkoutLog` (L427 deletes, L435 recalculates): when `log.enrollment_id` is set, delegate to `removeProgramSession`; otherwise the plain delete. `src/db/workoutRepo.ts` · `reviseWorkout` (L719-762): its read at L693 selects `id, enrollment_id, ended_at, started_at` — add `adjustments`, read the enrollment with `getEnrollmentById` for `unitSystem`, build the new rows, compute `replayedState({ replaceLogId, entries: entriesFromSets(newRows, log.adjustments, enr.unitSystem) })` **before any write**, then one rpc `replace_sets_and_replay`; the hand-rolled "read all sets, delete, insert, restore on failure" block goes. New `src/programs/errors.ts` · `class ProgramRefused extends Error` (a refusal the person can act on, as opposed to a failure) and `export function statusFor(e: unknown): number` (409 for `ProgramRefused`, 500 otherwise) — every repo function in this phase maps SQLSTATE 55000 from an rpc to `ProgramRefused`. Routes change their `catch` line only, so none grows past 50 code lines (`app/api/health/workout/route.ts` is at 46 today): `app/api/health/workout/route.ts` DELETE (L44-52) returns `{ recalculated }` and `err((e as Error).message, statusFor(e))` instead of the fixed "Failed to delete workout log" — so "That workout could not be deleted" is now true whenever it is shown (HistoryTab's copy and reload behaviour are Phase 7's; it relies on this step for the message being honest); `app/api/workouts/[id]/revise/route.ts` and `app/api/programs/enrollments/[id]/log/[logId]/route.ts` likewise. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/sessionEdits.test.ts` (new; mock `createServerSupabaseClient` the way `tests/unit/tracking/rollTrackingCounters.test.ts:19-24` does — `tests/helpers/fakeSupabase.ts` has no `rpc`, so it gains one: `rpc(name, args)` records the call and returns the `{ data, error }` the test configured) — "a delete whose recalculation throws writes nothing: no delete, no rpc"; "a delete that recalculates sends the workout id, the replayed state and the session count it read, in one rpc"; "a correction sends the new sets and the replayed state in one rpc and never deletes sets on its own"; "a stale session count from the database surfaces as ProgramRefused, and the route answers 409". `tests/e2e/programs-history-progress.spec.ts` (already in the `training` project) — "deleting a session on a program edited after it started moves the weights back and the row is gone": enrol `programId: "custom"` by `POST /api/programs/enrollments` with a two-lift `customSchedule` literal in the shape of `customProgram.ts:38-44` (`kind: "linear_rotation"`, one day, `scheme: { kind: "linear", sets: 5, reps: 5 }`, `progression: { kind: "linear_load", … }`) and `workingWeights`; `PUT …/schedule` with the same schedule plus a third lift and `workingWeights: { <id>: 20 }` (the body `EditActiveProgram.tsx:101-108` sends); finish two sessions through `/api/workouts` the way the existing "deleting a program session moves the weights back down" test does (L277-330); delete the first from the History tab; assert the row is gone and `/api/programs/enrollments/[id]` prescribes the pre-session weight for the first lift and still 20 for the added one.

4. **The whole history replays, however long it is.** `src/db/healthRepo.ts` · `finishedWorkouts` (L190) wraps a chain, and `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts:855-870` reads a chain only up to the wrapper's closing bracket — so a `.range()` placed after `finishedWorkouts(…)` is invisible to the guard and the count would not fall (verified by replicating the counter: today it flags exactly `healthRepo.ts:839, 869, 901` and `programRepo.ts:142, 164, 193, 204, 970`). Change: export `FINISHED_WORKOUTS_FILTER = "ended_at.not.is.null,started_at.is.null"` from healthRepo, `finishedWorkouts` becomes `query.or(FINISHED_WORKOUTS_FILTER)`, and every paged read below writes `.or(FINISHED_WORKOUTS_FILTER)` inline so `.range` sits in the same chain. `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `sessionLogsFor` (L963-975: `select("*, workout_sets(*)")`, no `.range`): `readAllRows` from `src/db/paging.ts` ordered by `logged_at` then `id`. · `listActiveEnrollments` (L163-170) and `listPastEnrollments` (L200-206) read every `workout_logs` row for the enrollments to find "last trained": the active list becomes one `.order("logged_at", desc).limit(1)` query per running program (there is at most one per discipline, so at most a handful — the N+1 the comment fears is bounded by the discipline count); the past list uses `readAllRows` over `(enrollment_id, logged_at)` ordered by `id`. `src/db/healthRepo.ts` · `getFlexibilityHoursCumulative` (L835), `getRunningDistanceCumulative` (L865), `getConsecutiveCardioWeeks` (L897): `readAllRows` ordered by `id`. `getLongestRunKm` (L880) already has `.limit(1)` — the finding counts it as a fourth unpaged read and is wrong on that one; it stays. `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` `UNPAGED_READS_ALLOWED` (L815): `programRepo.ts` 5 → 2 (the two remaining are the `program_enrollments` selects at L142 and L193), `healthRepo.ts` 9 → 6. *Test:* `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — "the unpaged-read allowance only shrinks" (fails until the numbers are lowered; "no NEW read…" fails if any is added back); `tests/unit/programs/sessionEdits.test.ts` — "a program with 1,001 sessions replays all of them" (the fake client returns a full page of 1,000 then one more; the replayed `sessionCount` is 1,001).

5. **Starting and ending programs cannot half-happen, and refuse while a workout is open.** `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `enrollInProgram` (L287-293 deactivates first; `requireProgram(e.program_id)` at L289 throws on any old enrollment whose catalogue id is gone): build the row (with the step-2 initial `schedule` event when a custom schedule is given), compute `displaced` with `getProgram` (`src/programs/data/catalog.ts:51`; an enrollment whose program is no longer in the catalogue is left alone and not displaced), call `start_enrollment` once; map SQLSTATE 55000 to `ProgramRefused`. · `resumeEnrollment` (L440-468, same two-step, and `requireProgram(target.program_id)` at L451): `getProgram` for the target — refuse with `ProgramRefused("That program is no longer in the catalogue, so it cannot be run again")` when absent — `getProgram` skipping unknowns in the displacement loop, then one rpc `resume_enrollment`. · `unenroll` (L379-387): `end_enrollment(id)`. `ensureEnrollment` (L328) and `programDraftRepo.startDraft` (`src/db/programDraftRepo.ts:218`) go through `enrollInProgram` and need no change. Routes `app/api/programs/enrollments/route.ts` POST, `[id]/route.ts` DELETE, `[id]/resume/route.ts`: `err((e as Error).message, statusFor(e))` (409 carries the sentence; everything else stays as it is). The three screens that POST a start today — `ProgramDetail.tsx:74-80`, `CustomProgramBuilder.tsx:217-236`, `src/goals/components/north-star/WorkoutPrograms.tsx:182-205` — already check `res.ok` and show `body.error`, so the refusal sentence reaches them with no client change (verified by reading each). *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/programRepo.enroll.test.ts` (new, fake client with `rpc`) — "a start hands the new row and the programs it displaces to one rpc and never updates is_active itself"; "a start with its own schedule records that schedule as the first replay event"; "an old enrollment whose program left the catalogue does not stop a start"; "ending a program the open workout belongs to surfaces the server's refusal as ProgramRefused". `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — new test "is_active is only ever changed by the database functions" (no `is_active: true|false` inside an `.update(`/`.insert(` payload anywhere in `src/db/programRepo.ts`; the other repos that write `is_active` — `trackingRepo.ts`, `goalRepo.ts` — are other tables and are out of scope, so the test is scoped to `program_enrollments` writers). Integration tests from step 1 prove the SQL side.

6. **One owner for program actions on the client.** New `src/programs/programActions.ts` · `endProgram(id)` (DELETE `/api/programs/enrollments/[id]`), `skipSession(id)` and `resetProgram(id)` (POST `…/action`), `restartProgram(id)` (POST `…/resume`), `deletePastProgram(id)` (DELETE `…?permanent=1`) — each does the fetch, checks `res.ok`, reads `body.error`, and returns `{ ok: true, data } | { ok: false, error: string }` with the sentence "Could not reach the server, so nothing was changed." for a thrown fetch; none of them navigates or refreshes. Today: `ProgressionView.unenroll` (`src/programs/components/ProgressionView.tsx:107-108`), `ProgramsApp.onFinish` (`src/programs/components/ProgramsApp.tsx:349-359`: `"archive"` is an end, `"restart"` is a **reset** — `action: "reset"`, not `/resume`) and `RunningPrograms.end` (`src/programs/components/RunningPrograms.tsx:67`) `await fetch(...)` and carry on; only `ProgressionView.action` checks. Change: those call sites use `endProgram` / `resetProgram` / `skipSession`, and `PastPrograms.resume` / `erase` (`src/programs/components/PastPrograms.tsx:96`, `:144`) use `restartProgram` / `deletePastProgram`; on `ok: false` they stay where they are and show the sentence in their existing `failed`/`error` slot (ProgressionView: `data-testid="progression-action-failed"`; ProgramsApp gets one `data-testid="finish-action-failed"` line under SessionNotices; RunningPrograms gets `data-testid="running-end-failed"`). ProgressionView is replaced by the program sheet in Phase 5 (S2 spec, element 7) — the sheet must use this module, which is why the rule lives in a module and not in the component. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/programActions.test.ts` — "endProgram returns the server's sentence on a 409 and never reports success"; "a lost connection is reported as could-not-reach, not as done"; `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — new test "program actions go through programActions.ts" (no `fetch(` whose URL contains `/api/programs/enrollments/` together with `method: "DELETE"`, `/action`, or `/resume` in `src/**` or `components/**` outside that module; allowlist empty); `tests/unit/programs/ProgressionView.test.tsx` (new) — "End program stays on the program and shows the reason when the server refuses" (this component test dies with ProgressionView in Phase 5; the module test and the architecture test are what survive).

7. **Skip means something, or it is not offered — and it asks first.** `src/programs/programsService.ts` · new `skipRefusal(schedule: ProgramSchedule): string | null` — returns "This week runs by the calendar, so there is nothing to skip. Wednesday's session is Wednesday's whether or not you did it." when `isWeekdayAnchored(schedule)` (L162; false for endurance and in-order programs, so those keep Skip), else `null`. `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `skipSession` (L634-650): today advances the cursor and records a `skip` event even though `getTodaySession` ignores the cursor on a calendar week (L606-620), so the screen does not change and each tap adds a phantom skip. Change: throw `ProgramRefused(skipRefusal(program.schedule))` first; `app/api/programs/enrollments/[id]/action/route.ts` uses `statusFor`. `src/programs/components/ProgressionView.tsx` (L272-274; it already resolves `program` at L64 via `effectiveProgram(requireProgram(...), enrollment.customSchedule)`): the Skip button is not rendered when `skipRefusal(program.schedule)` is non-null, and otherwise confirms with "Skip Workout B? The program moves on to Workout A as if today's session had happened. Your weights do not change." (uses `dayLabel`). Phase 5's sheet row "Skip this session" uses the same `skipRefusal` (dependency). *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/enginePhase0.test.ts` — "a week pinned to weekdays has nothing to skip, and an in-order program does"; `tests/unit/programs/ProgressionView.test.tsx` — "Skip asks first, and is not offered on a calendar week".

8. **Reset says what it does — the words are derived from the event.** Gated on **Open question 2**. `src/programs/programsService.ts` · new `export const RESET_EFFECT = { cursor: true, weights: false } as const` and `resetConfirmText(effect = RESET_EFFECT)` → "Start again from week 1? Your weights stay where they are — to start from your original weights, end this program and start it again." when `weights` is false, and "…Your weights go back to where you began. This cannot be undone." when true. `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `resetEnrollment` (L480-501) writes `{ at, kind: "reset", ...RESET_EFFECT }` instead of the literal at L491. `src/programs/components/ProgressionView.tsx` (L284) and its comment (L275-278, which describes a weight reset that does not happen) use `resetConfirmText()`; Phase 5's sheet row uses the same function (dependency). If the owner chooses the other product (weights reset too): `RESET_EFFECT.weights` becomes `true`, `ReplayEvent`'s reset member (`types.ts:462`, literal `weights: false`) widens to `weights: boolean`, `resetEnrollment` re-seeds `exercise_state` from `initial_exercise_state`, and the replay branch at `programsService.ts:1865` restores the seed when `event.weights` — the copy follows automatically. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/enginePhase0.test.ts` — "the reset confirm says weights stay when the event keeps them, and would say they go back if it did not" (asserts both branches of `resetConfirmText`); `tests/unit/programs/sessionEdits.test.ts` — "a reset writes exactly RESET_EFFECT into the replay event" (fake client); the existing "a reset rewinds the plan and keeps the weights, as the button does" stays green.

9. **One "today" for the strip and the card, from the account's clock.** `src/shared/dateUtils.ts` · `periodStartInTimezone` (L156) takes no instant, so a fixed-instant test cannot be written against it — gains `at: Date = new Date()` as a third parameter, like its siblings `getTodayInTimezone` (L23) and `isoWeekdayInTimezone` (L102); no caller changes. `src/programs/programsService.ts` · new pure `weekSoFar(logs: { logged_at: string }[], timezone: string, now: Date): { todayWeekday: number; trainedWeekdays: number[] }` — a log is this week's when `getTodayInTimezone(timezone, new Date(l.logged_at)) >= periodStartInTimezone("weekly", timezone, now)`, and its weekday is `isoWeekdayInTimezone(timezone, new Date(l.logged_at))`. `src/programs/types.ts` · `SessionPrescription` (L565) gains `clockKnown: boolean` (one owner: step 10's `getUserClock`; every screen that has a prescription has the fact); `EnrollmentDetail` (L811) gains `week: { todayWeekday; trainedWeekdays }`. `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `getTodaySession` (L592-621) reads the timezone once via `getUserClock` and stamps `clockKnown`; new `getEnrollmentDetail(userId, id): Promise<EnrollmentDetail | null>` — one clock read, the prescription, the logs (`sessionLogsFor`), and `weekSoFar(logs, timezone, new Date())`. Both callers that build the detail by hand today use it: `app/api/programs/enrollments/[id]/route.ts` GET (L8-19) and `app/programs/page.tsx:52-58` (the server-rendered first paint — without this the page's `initialDetail` has no `week` and the strip is empty until the first refetch). Today the client works the same facts out three times from the phone's clock: `WeekStrip.tsx:82` `isoWeekday(new Date())`, `ProgramsApp.tsx:231` `periodStartFor("weekly", new Date())`, `ProgramsApp.tsx:325-327` per-log weekday in the browser's zone — while the server already sends `prescription.todayWeekday` (`types.ts:620`), which no component reads. Change: `WeekStrip` takes `today: number` and `trainedWeekdays` as props and drops the `isoWeekday` import (L40); `ProgramsApp` passes `detail.week.todayWeekday` / `detail.week.trainedWeekdays` and deletes the `weekStartedOn` memo and the `periodStartFor`/`toDateISO` (L4) and `isoWeekday` (L22) imports (only those lines — the rest of ProgramsApp is Phase 5's). Not closed here, named so it is not silent: `ProgressTab.tsx:92` `const now = new Date()` feeds `adherenceThisWeek` and `weeklyVolume` — the Progress tab's "This week" is still on the browser clock; it is the Progress tab rebuild's (Phase 5, S2 element 20) and the guard below holds it at exactly one until then. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/weekSoFar.test.ts` — "a Sunday 23:30 workout in Auckland is this week there and last week's in London" and "today's weekday comes from the account's zone, not the process's" (fixed instants, two zones); `tests/unit/programs/WeekStrip.test.tsx` (new; WeekStrip survives into S2 per spec element 8) — "highlights the weekday the server names even when the browser's clock says another"; `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — new test "no calendar fact is read from the browser clock in src/programs/components" (counts bare `new Date()` per file under `src/programs/components/**`; allowance `{ "ProgressTab.tsx": 1, "live/FinishSheet.tsx": 1, "LiftHistory.tsx": 1 }` — the last two are instants, an end time and a download filename, not calendar facts — with the paired "only shrinks" test; `WeekStrip.tsx` and `ProgramsApp.tsx` are deliberately absent so the test fails until they are clean).

10. **The app knows whether it knows your clock.** Gated on **Open questions 3, 4 and 7**. `supabase/migrations/20260917110000_timezone_source.sql` (new): `alter table profiles add column timezone_source text not null default 'signup_default' check (timezone_source in ('signup_default','detected','chosen'))`; backfill `update profiles set timezone_source = 'chosen' where timezone <> 'UTC'` (a non-UTC value can only have been typed; a UTC value is treated as never set — Open question 7); `grant update (timezone_source) on profiles to authenticated` — **ASK FIRST** (the column allow-list from `20260828140001_profiles_rls_hardening.sql:34-43` is the precedent); and `handle_new_user()` (`20260101000000_create_profiles.sql:135-150`) additionally writes `timezone` and `timezone_source` from `new.raw_user_meta_data->>'timezone'` **only when** `exists (select 1 from pg_timezone_names where name = <that value>)` — otherwise the defaults stay — **ASK FIRST** (it is the signup trigger). The validation is not optional: without it a tampered or malformed signup writes any string into `profiles.timezone`, and `toZonedDate` (`dateUtils.ts:50`) then falls back to UTC for that account on every call, silently, which is exactly the class of fault this step exists to remove. `src/db/settingsRepo.ts` · `getUserTimezone` (L153-176) today returns the stored value and trusts the default. Change: new `getUserClock(userId): Promise<{ timezone: string; known: boolean }>` (`known = timezone_source !== 'signup_default'`); `getUserTimezone` delegates to it (its callers in 26 files are unchanged); `updateTimezone(userId, timezone, source: 'detected' | 'chosen')` writes both columns in **one** `.update({ timezone, timezone_source })`; `getTimePreferences` (L207) also returns `timezone_source`. `src/settings/settingsService.ts` · `handleUpdateTimezone(userId, timezone, source = 'chosen')` — `src/settings/actions.ts:84` (the Settings page's server action) and `GoalTimeSettingsDialog.tsx:109` (the PUT route) both land on the default and so record `chosen`. `app/api/settings/time-preferences/route.ts` PUT accepts optional `source` (`'detected'` passes through; anything else is `'chosen'`). `app/auth/sign-up/page.tsx` (L49-58) adds `timezone: Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone` to `options.data`. New `components/ClockSync.tsx` mounted once in `app/layout.tsx` (beside `ViewportHeightUpdater`, L73 — the root layout is the only shell every signed-in page shares; signed-out pages get a 401 and do nothing): once per browser session (a `sessionStorage` flag, every read and write in try/catch), `GET /api/settings/time-preferences`; on 401 do nothing; when `timezone_source === 'signup_default'` and the browser zone is valid and differs, `PUT { timezone, source: 'detected' }`; never writes when the source is `chosen` or `detected`. Rendering the line "Days are counted in UTC until you set your timezone — Settings ›" on the Tracking card and the Today card is Phase 3's (S1) and Phase 5's (S2) work, and both must read `prescription.clockKnown` rather than re-deriving it (dependency — named so the fact has one owner). `tests/e2e/auth.setup.ts` and `auth-user-b.setup.ts`: after `waitForURL`, `page.request.put('/api/settings/time-preferences', { data: { timezone: 'UTC', source: 'chosen' } })` for account A and `{ timezone: 'Europe/Copenhagen', source: 'chosen' }` for B — the sync may fire once on the login landing before the pin, and the pin then wins and stops it for good, so the fixtures the walk and the training specs depend on can never drift (today neither setup nor `playwright.config.ts` pins a zone). `tests/integration/schema.sql` gains the column, its check and — because that file has `timezone TEXT` with no default (L59) — `not null default 'UTC'` to match production. Proving the trigger: the integration schema has no `handle_new_user` and its `createTestUser` inserts into `profiles` directly (`tests/integration/setup.ts:171`), so no integration test can exercise it. The executor signs up a throwaway address (`phase1-clock-<date>@daygame-coach-test.local`) on the dev server after the migration is applied and reads back `begin; select timezone, timezone_source from profiles where email = '<it>'; rollback;` — a signup through the app is one of the two allowed ways to write live data — and records the result in the attack log; the account stays unless the owner removes it in the Supabase dashboard. *Test:* `tests/integration/db/settingsRepo.integration.test.ts` — "a new profile's clock is the signup default and says so" (column default); "a zone the person chose is never the signup default" (`updateTimezone(…, 'chosen')` writes both columns); "the source column refuses a value it does not know" (the CHECK); `tests/unit/settings/clockSync.test.tsx` (new) — "writes the browser's zone once when the account still holds the signup default"; "never overwrites a zone the person chose"; "does nothing when signed out"; `tests/unit/settings/timezoneSource.test.ts` (new) — "the settings action and the goals dialog record a chosen zone; only the sync records a detected one" (asserts `handleUpdateTimezone`'s default and the route's mapping).

11. **The write-up route cannot double-log, cannot half-write, and keeps the all-out set.** Gated on **Open question 5**. `src/programs/schemas.ts` · `LogSessionSchema` (L224, `.strict()`, no key) gains `clientKey: z.string().min(8).max(64)` (required, like `StartWorkoutSchema:286`); the fixture `body` in `tests/unit/programs/logSessionSchema.test.ts:16-23` gains one so its six tests stay green. `src/programs/components/TodaySessionWidget.tsx` (L160-195): mint one key per form instance with `newClientKey` from `src/programs/hooks/useLiveWorkout.ts:453` (a `useRef`, rotated after a success), send it, and change the catch copy at L191 from "Nothing was recorded." to "Could not confirm the save — check History before trying again." (this file is deleted in Phase 4 under Decision 3; the change is ten lines). `src/db/programRepo.ts` · `logProgramSession` (L668-711) and `writeWorkout` (L849-891): today `writeWorkout` is called without `clientKey` (L706) and inserts the workout, then the sets, then `persistState` runs — three writes. Change: `logProgramSession` computes the result, then one rpc `log_session_and_advance` (step 1) with the workout row, the set rows and the replayed state; when the rpc answers `inserted: false` it returns the current prescription **without** advancing; `writeWorkout` is deleted (its only caller was this). · `setRowsFor` (L933, always `set_kind: "working"`): write `set_kind: "amrap"` when the prescribed set for that `setNumber` on that day carries `amrap: true` (from `computePrescription` for `logInput.dayId`); the live screen already does this (`live/LiveWorkoutScreen.tsx:389`), so History stops disagreeing between the two paths. `app/api/programs/enrollments/[id]/log/route.ts`: `statusFor` in its catch. *Test:* `tests/unit/programs/logSessionSchema.test.ts` — "refuses a write-up without a retry key"; `tests/unit/programs/sessionEdits.test.ts` — "logging a write-up sends the workout, its sets and the advanced state in one rpc"; "the same write-up sent twice advances the program once" (fake rpc returns `inserted: false` the second time); "an all-out set written up after the fact is stored as amrap". Integration: step 1's "the same write-up sent twice records one workout and advances the program once".

12. **The training specs stop ending programs over an open workout.** `tests/e2e/programs-drafts.spec.ts:111` and `tests/e2e/life-mastery-saved-weeks.spec.ts:163,197` end enrollments in their cleanup without first deleting a live workout (the other five training specs do: `programs-live-workout.spec.ts:31-32`, `dashboard-training-card.spec.ts:22-23`, `programs-offline.spec.ts:28-29`, …). After step 5 that end is refused with 409 whenever the previous spec left a workout open, and the cleanup silently leaves the program running. Change: both cleanups gain the same two lines (`GET /api/workouts/live`, `DELETE /api/workouts/<id>`) before the enrollment loop. *Test:* the `training` project itself — run it end to end after step 5 and again at the end; a refused cleanup shows as the next spec seeing an unexpected running program.

### Findings in scope that are deliberately not fixed here
None dropped. Two related findings sit outside this phase's scope and are named so nobody thinks they were forgotten: **history-progress-06** (the delete confirm does not warn that the weights move) is Phase 7's copy; **data-integrity-05** (a signed-in person can write `exercise_state` / `cursor` directly through the REST API, bypassing every guard above because the new functions are `SECURITY INVOKER` like `finish_program_workout`) is a security decision — see Open question 6 — and is not changed by this phase. One leftover of the same class as programs-today-08 is named in step 9 (`ProgressTab.tsx:92`) and held at one by the new guard.

### Acceptance
- `tests/integration/db/programRepo.integration.test.ts` — the four new tests prove, against a real Postgres, that ending/displacing with an open workout is refused, a refused insert leaves the old program running, a stale session count is refused with the session intact, and a repeated write-up is a no-op. (RLS is not on in the integration schema — data-integrity-07 — so "runs as the signed-in user" is proven by the `SECURITY INVOKER` declaration and by `security-rls.spec.ts`, not here.)
- `tests/unit/programs/enginePhase0.test.ts` — four replay tests prove a schedule (the starting one and every edit) is part of the history and the cursor lands on a real day; "a week pinned to weekdays has nothing to skip"; the reset copy is derived from `RESET_EFFECT`.
- `tests/unit/programs/sessionEdits.test.ts` — delete/correct/log write nothing on a failed recalculation and one rpc on success; a stale count is a 409; 1,001 sessions replay in full; a repeated write-up advances once; AMRAP kind kept; a reset writes `RESET_EFFECT`.
- `tests/unit/programs/programRepo.enroll.test.ts` — one rpc per start/resume/end; a custom start records its schedule; unknown catalogue ids do not abort a start; refusals surface as `ProgramRefused`.
- `tests/unit/programs/programActions.test.ts` + `ProgressionView.test.tsx` — every program action reports the server's sentence and never claims success; Skip asks first and is hidden on a calendar week.
- `tests/unit/programs/weekSoFar.test.ts` + `WeekStrip.test.tsx` — today and this week's trained days come from the account's zone; the strip highlights what the server names.
- `tests/unit/settings/clockSync.test.tsx`, `timezoneSource.test.ts`, `tests/integration/db/settingsRepo.integration.test.ts` — the clock is "unknown" until detected or chosen; the sync writes once and never over a chosen zone; the source column refuses unknown values. The signup trigger is proven by the throwaway signup and the read-only probe recorded in the attack log.
- `tests/e2e/programs-history-progress.spec.ts` (training project) — deleting a session on an edited program moves the weights back, keeps the added lift's weight and removes the row, read back from the API.
- `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` — four new tests ("program actions go through programActions.ts", "is_active is only ever changed by the database functions", "custom_schedule is written in one place", "no calendar fact is read from the browser clock in src/programs/components" with its paired "only shrinks") plus `UNPAGED_READS_ALLOWED` lowered to `programRepo.ts: 2`, `healthRepo.ts: 6`.
- Every step is done only when its named test passes **and** fails with the change reverted (the executor reverts once per step and records the failure in the attack log).
- Guards that must stay green: `tests/unit/architecture.test.ts` (all sections — API routes under 50 code lines: every route in this phase changes its `catch` line only, via `statusFor`), `tests/unit/navigation/routeReachability.test.ts`, `tests/unit/navigation/backNavigation.test.ts`, `tests/unit/writeCoverage.test.ts` (the new repo functions `removeProgramSession`, `replayedState`, `getEnrollmentDetail`, `getUserClock` and the changed `updateTimezone`, `enrollInProgram`, `resumeEnrollment`, `unenroll`, `logProgramSession`, `resetEnrollment`, `skipSession` must be classified in `tests/support/writeCoverage.baseline.json`; the fake-client tests above assert the payload handed to the database, so they count as "asserted" and `MAX_UNASSERTED` in `writeCoverage.test.ts` goes **down**, never up; entries for the deleted `recalculateEnrollment`, `reviseSessionLog`'s old body and `writeWorkout` are removed). Allowlists may shrink; none may grow. `npm test` after every step; `npm run test:integration` after steps 1, 2, 3, 5, 10; the `training` Playwright project after steps 3, 5 and 12 and again at the end.

### Attack log
- Step 2 started every replay from schedule `null` (the catalogue). A program enrolled *with* its own schedule — every self-built week, whose catalogue entry `custom` is one placeholder day (`customProgram.ts:28-44`) — would replay its first sessions against that placeholder and throw. Fixed: enrolment records an initial `schedule` event; the legacy repair covers rows that predate it.
- Step 2 changed `replayEnrollment`'s signature; `tests/unit/programs/engine.test.ts` calls it twelve times with the old one. Fixed: signature kept, meaning carried by the events.
- Step 2 did not clamp the cursor when a schedule event shortens the day list; a later session logged on a removed day falls back to the cursor index (`applyLog` L605-606) and `dayAt` throws mid-replay — the same crash the step exists to remove. Fixed: clamp on every schedule event, with a named test.
- Step 2 left tie order between a schedule event and the weight events of the same save unstated. Fixed: schedule appended first, stable sort, said in a comment.
- Step 4's paged reads would not lower the count: `finishedWorkouts(...)` wraps the chain and the guard's `chainAt` (architecture.test.ts L855-870) stops at the wrapper's bracket, so a trailing `.range()` is invisible. Verified by replicating the counter (flags `healthRepo.ts:839, 869, 901`, `programRepo.ts:142, 164, 193, 204, 970` today). Fixed: `FINISHED_WORKOUTS_FILTER` inline in the paged chains.
- Step 9 named `periodStartInTimezone`, which has no instant parameter, so the fixed-instant test could not exist. Fixed: widened with a defaulted `at`.
- Step 9 missed `app/programs/page.tsx:52-58`, which builds the detail by hand for the first paint; `detail.week` would be undefined and the strip empty until a refetch. Fixed: both callers use `getEnrollmentDetail`.
- Step 9's guard patterns missed `ProgressTab.tsx:92`, a fourth browser-clock calendar read of the same class. Fixed: the guard counts bare `new Date()` with a shrinking allowance; ProgressTab is named as the Progress-tab phase's, not hidden.
- Step 9 put `clockKnown` only on the detail; `TrainingCard.tsx:129-133` reads `detail.prescription`, so S1 would have had to re-derive it. Fixed: `clockKnown` lives on `SessionPrescription`.
- Step 5 overloaded `start_enrollment` with a `p_resume_id` so one function did two jobs, and `resumeEnrollment` still called `requireProgram` (L451) on the target. Fixed: separate `resume_enrollment`, `getProgram` with a refusal sentence.
- Step 3's `reviseWorkout` cannot build `entriesFromSets(newRows, log.adjustments, unit)` as written: its read (`workoutRepo.ts:693-698`) selects no `adjustments` and nothing reads the unit. Fixed: select widened, enrollment read.
- Steps 3, 5, 7, 11 add `instanceof` branches to routes; `app/api/health/workout/route.ts` is at 46 code lines under the guard's counter. Fixed: `statusFor(e)` in `errors.ts`, every route changes its catch line only.
- Step 10's trigger wrote whatever string the sign-up page sent into `profiles.timezone`; a bad value makes `toZonedDate` fall back to UTC on every call for that account, silently. Fixed: validated against `pg_timezone_names`, defaults otherwise.
- Step 10's backfill turns a genuinely chosen UTC into "never set", and the sync would then overwrite it once. Not fixable by code (the fact was never stored) — made an open question (7) with the fixtures pinned either way.
- Step 10's integration test "a new profile's clock is the signup default" could not prove the trigger: `schema.sql:59` has `timezone TEXT` nullable with no default and no `handle_new_user`, and `createTestUser` inserts into profiles directly. Fixed: column default proven by integration, trigger proven by a throwaway signup and a read-only probe, both named.
- Step 10's sync in the root layout runs on signed-out pages. Kept there (there is no shared signed-in layout — `app/dashboard/tracking/layout.tsx` and `app/life-mastery/layout.tsx` are per-area), with the 401 branch and try/catch around `sessionStorage` spelled out.
- Step 11 makes `clientKey` required; `tests/unit/programs/logSessionSchema.test.ts:16-23` would break. Fixed: fixture named.
- The fake-client tests assumed `tests/helpers/fakeSupabase.ts` can record an rpc; it has no `rpc` (and no unit test asserts an rpc payload today — `finishWorkout`'s "asserted" is by mention only). Fixed: helper extended, named in touches.
- Step 5's refusal breaks two training specs' cleanups (`programs-drafts.spec.ts:111`, `life-mastery-saved-weeks.spec.ts:163,197` end programs without deleting an open workout first). Fixed: step 12.
- Step 6 described ProgramsApp's "restart" as a `/resume`; it is `action: "reset"` (`ProgramsApp.tsx:354-358`). Fixed: mapped explicitly. The three start POSTs (`ProgramDetail.tsx:74`, `CustomProgramBuilder.tsx:217`, `WorkoutPrograms.tsx:182`) were checked and already surface `body.error`, so the 409 sentence reaches them unchanged.

**Found while building it, 18 September (steps 6–12).**
- Step 11's duplicate branch returned `result.changes` — the list of weight movements the screen prints verbatim. `result` is computed by advancing from the state as it stands, which the FIRST copy of the write-up has already advanced, so a retry would have shown "Squat 62.5 → 65 kg" when the earlier request moved it 60 → 62.5 and this one moved nothing. Exactly the class of lie this phase removes. Fixed: `{ enrollment: enr, changes: [], next: <the prescription it actually gives> }`, with a named test.
- Step 9's `getEnrollmentDetail` read the account's clock twice — once itself and once inside `getTodaySession`. Two reads of the fact whose single-sourcing is the entire point of the step. Fixed: one read, passed into `todaysSessionFor`; `sessionLogsFor` used directly with the enrollment already in hand, which also removes a third `getEnrollmentById`.
- Step 11's `setRowsFor` first read the amrap flags off the cursor's day. A session written up three days late is Workout B's whatever the program points at now, so the all-out set would have been marked on the wrong lift. Fixed: the day being logged decides.
- `tests/unit/settings/clockSync.test.tsx` passed with an arrow function as the `Intl.DateTimeFormat` mock — an arrow cannot be constructed, so `browserZone()` threw, the component gave up, and six tests passed by the component doing nothing. Caught because the one test that asserted a PUT failed. Fixed: a plain `function`, and the reason is written above the mock.
- Step 10's trigger could not be proven by any existing harness (no `auth.users` in `tests/integration/schema.sql`, and `createTestUser` writes `profiles` directly). The plan's answer was a throwaway signup against the live database. Done better: `auth.users`, `handle_new_user` and the trigger are built inside the integration container, exercised with a real zone, four forged ones (including `'; drop table profiles; --`) and no zone at all, then dropped. No live account created, and the `pg_timezone_names` gate is proven rather than assumed.
- The lint ratchet OOMs on this machine at ~4 GB — in the eslint CHILD process, so `node --max-old-space-size` on the wrapper does nothing. It has to be `NODE_OPTIONS`. Worth knowing before anybody reads a crash as a lint failure; the ratchet itself is correct and refuses to treat non-JSON output as a pass.
- Step 1 claimed atomicity; what it cannot make unrepresentable is two tabs starting two different strength programs at once (discipline lives in code). Stated in the step rather than implied away.
- Opening called the default "London time"; UTC is not London for half the year. Reworded, and it now says no screen changes in this phase.
- Checked and found sound: `ON CONFLICT (user_id, client_key) WHERE client_key IS NOT NULL` matches the partial index at `20260907100000:115`; `authenticated` exists in `schema.sql:594` so the GRANTs copy verbatim; `isWeekdayAnchored` is false for endurance so Skip stays for cursor-driven plans; `getLongestRunKm` already has `.limit(1)`; `updateTimezone` writes both columns in one statement; the `settings/actions.ts:84` path defaults to `chosen`.

### Open questions for Phase 1

Numbered as the steps above refer to them. Each carries a recommendation.

**Open question 1.** Step 1 lets signed-in users run five new database functions (end a program, start one, resume one, remove a session and move the weights, replace a session's sets and move the weights, write up a session and move the weights). Each runs as the signed-in person under the existing row rules — exactly how the existing finish_program_workout is set up — and no row-security policy is added or changed. Because a GRANT is a permission, the rules say ask first: may these five functions be granted to signed-in users?

*Recommendation:* Yes. It is the same pattern already in production for finishing a workout, and it is the only way to make a delete-plus-recalculation happen together or not at all.

**Open question 2.** "Reset to start" today rewinds the program to week 1 and KEEPS your current weights, while the warning says your weights go back to where you began. Which should it be: (a) keep the weights and change the words to say so, or (b) make it also put the weights back to your starting numbers?

*Recommendation:* (a) Keep the weights and tell the truth — the engine, its replay and its existing test all already do (a); anyone who wants a genuine fresh start can end the program and start it again, and the new confirm text says exactly that. If you choose (b), step 8 says what changes and it is about the same size.

**Open question 3.** To know whether the app actually knows your timezone (as opposed to the signup default of UTC), step 10 adds a small 'where did this zone come from' column to the profile and lets a signed-in person update that one column on their own row. May that column be added and that update permission granted?

*Recommendation:* Yes. Without it the app cannot tell 'this person chose UTC' from 'nobody ever set it', and the only alternative is guessing from the value 'UTC' itself.

**Open question 4.** Step 10 also changes the small database routine that creates a profile when someone signs up, so it records the phone's timezone at that moment (only if it is a real timezone name; anything else is ignored). It is part of the signup path, so: may the signup trigger be changed to read the timezone the sign-up page sends?

*Recommendation:* Yes. It is one extra field read from the same place the trigger already reads the person's name, and it means a new account never runs on the wrong clock even for a minute.

**Open question 5.** The 'Log a workout you already did' form is due to be deleted in Phase 4 (design Decision 3). Step 11 spends roughly 60 lines making its save route safe now (a retry cannot log the session twice or move the weights twice, and a half-written session cannot be left behind). Do you want that done now, or should the route simply be deleted in Phase 4 and left as it is until then?

*Recommendation:* Do it now. The plan is executed end to end, but every phase is checked and attacked on its own, and a route that can double-advance your program should not exist in any intermediate state; the one-statement function it uses is also the pattern the rest of the phase is built on.

**Open question 6.** Security, not fixed in this phase: with a valid login, a person can still write their own program's weights, cursor and 'finished' state straight into the database through the public API, bypassing every guard this phase adds (finding data-integrity-05). Only their own account can be affected, but records, streaks and 1RM tiles are then self-reported. Do you want that closed (a column allow-list plus a database-owned function, the way profiles was hardened on 2026-08-28), or documented and accepted as personal data the person may edit?

*Recommendation:* Close it, in its own small phase after Phase 1, because every guard written here is advisory until then. It is not in Phase 1 because it is a permissions change and needs its own ask.

**Open question 7.** Existing accounts whose timezone is exactly 'UTC' cannot be told apart from accounts nobody ever set. Step 10 treats every 'UTC' as 'never set', so the next time such a person opens the app on a phone in, say, New York, their zone is quietly set to New York once (and never touched again). Anyone who genuinely chose UTC would have to choose it again in Settings. The two test accounts are pinned either way. Is that acceptable, or should existing accounts all be treated as 'chosen' so nothing changes for anyone who already exists?

*Recommendation:* Accept it. A real person on UTC by choice is rare and the correction is one tap; a real person on UTC by accident is the bug this step exists to fix, and treating everyone as 'chosen' would leave every one of them on the wrong clock for good.


### Blockers for Phase 1

1. **How many existing programs were edited after they started (or started with their own schedule) without that being recorded — the rows step 2's repair exists for — is not known.** *Attempted:* Not run in this attack pass either: the session was told not to touch the database. The count needs a read-only probe wrapped in a rollback: begin; select count(*) from program_enrollments where custom_schedule is not null and not (replay_events @> '[{"kind":"schedule"}]'); rollback; *Needs from you:* Nothing to decide — the executor runs that read-only probe as part of step 2 and writes the number in the attack log; the repair code is written either way. *Recommendation:* Let the executor run it; no action needed from you.


## Phase 2 — Life Mastery and the training database stop disagreeing
**What you can do after:** Start a program — or a week you wrote yourself, bodyweight lifts included — from Life Mastery, and every screen tells the same story under the same name: the Systems step, the Templates block, the Tracking card, the Training page, the workout header and History all read from the one running program, on your phone and your laptop alike. Typing a day name works like typing (spaces included), a half-built program survives leaving the step, an edit made after you started either updates the running program or says plainly that it has not, and a finished workout ticks the Track step by itself. What does NOT change yet: the Templates step keeps its current look and still hosts the catalogue and editor inline (Phase 8 moves them to the Training page, per design spec S4), and the new "Change on the Training page" link lands on `/programs` whose Back returns you to Life Mastery only once Phase 5 wires the return address it already carries.
**Why it is in this order:** Phases 3, 5 and 8 build screens on top of the link between the plan and the running program; if the plan still keeps its own copy of the days and the name, every one of those screens inherits two versions of one fact.
**Findings this phase closes:** lm-templates-01, lm-templates-02, lm-templates-03, lm-templates-04, lm-templates-05, lm-templates-07, lm-templates-10, lm-templates-11, lm-templates-12, lm-build-own-01, lm-build-own-02, lm-build-own-03, lm-build-own-04, lm-build-own-05, lm-build-own-06, lm-build-own-07, lm-build-own-08, critic-systems-step-edits-days-the-program-never-hears-about, critic-track-step-does-not-know-you-trained, critic-live-header-calls-your-week-your-own-program, walk-08-plan-is-per-browser

**Left to other phases (in this phase's files, deliberately not fixed here):**
- lm-templates-06 (a start that fails halfway switches off the program you were on) — Phase 1's scope names it ("a start that fails halfway leaving the previous program switched off"). One fact Phase 1 must know, verified here: `supabase/migrations/20260618_create_program_tables.sql:30` has a partial unique index `uq_program_enrollments_active ON program_enrollments(user_id, program_id) WHERE is_active`. So "insert first, deactivate second" fails on the insert whenever the new row's `program_id` is already running — which is every second self-built week (all are `program_id = "custom"`), every restart of the same program, and step 16's "Start a fresh copy". The honest fix is one database function in a migration that deactivates and inserts in one transaction; a compensating re-activate on insert failure is the patch. Phase 0 step 5's `assertNoOpenWorkoutOn` sits before the loop and composes with either.
- tests-and-ci-04, tests-and-ci-11 — Phase 10 owns every `tests-and-ci-*` id. The browser specs that close them are written HERE (steps 24–25) because they are this phase's proof; Phase 10 will find them done and lists the ids.
- lm-templates-08, lm-templates-09, lm-build-own-10, lm-build-own-11, design-system-08, walk-06-templates-step-12000px, walk-07-day-name-truncated-by-icon-buttons — the skin of the Templates block and the editor rows; spec S4 replaces those surfaces outright (Phase 8) and Phase 9 removes the blue-grey kit, so repainting them here would be thrown away.
- lm-build-own-12 (Templates reopens on the catalogue) — S4 deletes the "ready-made / build my own" switch itself (Phase 8); with the switch gone there is nothing to reopen on. Step 20's draft hook records `mode` so Phase 8 has it if the owner keeps a switch.
- critic-end-or-displace-program-while-workout-open — Phase 0 step 5; nothing here touches `enrollInProgram`.

**The one rule this phase installs:** the plan in the browser stores *which* program the training week follows (an enrollment id) and nothing else about it. Day names, the week's shape, the program's name, whether it is still running and whether you trained — all of it is read live from the database through one shared hook and one describing function. Nothing about a program is copied into localStorage any more, so a phone and a laptop cannot hold different weeks, the Systems step cannot edit days the program never hears about, and the false "your written week is not the week any of these prescribe" warning has nothing left to compare. (Design spec S4 §b8–9 and Decision 11; open question 1 confirms it.) Verified while attacking: today `loadNsPlan` (`src/goals/northStarService.ts:520–575`) rebuilds every routine without its `program` field, so the reference is written to localStorage and then dropped on the very next reload — the plan has never actually remembered which program it follows past one page load. Step 7 fixes that as part of shrinking the reference.

**What survives Phase 8, said per step:** Phase 8 (spec S4, Decisions 5 and 11) deletes `BuildYourOwn.tsx`, `SavedWeeks.tsx`, the inline editor in `WorkoutPrograms.tsx` and — if Decision 5 is yes — the tap-to-build `CustomProgramBuilder`.
